title="Country code top-level domain">Internet TLD</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/.rw" title=".rw">.rw</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Rwanda</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/u/: &#39;u&#39; in &#39;influence&#39;">u</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="&#39;d&#39; in &#39;dye&#39;">d</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/2/2c/Rwanda_pronunciation.ogg/Rwanda_pronunciation.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:Rwanda_pronunciation.ogg" title="File:Rwanda pronunciation.ogg">i</a></sup></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling">roo-<span style="font-size:90%">AHN</span>-də</i></a> or <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/uː/: &#39;oo&#39; in &#39;goose&#39;">uː</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="&#39;d&#39; in &#39;dye&#39;">d</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling">roo-<span style="font-size:90%">AN</span>-də</i></a>; <a href="/wiki/Kinyarwanda_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Kinyarwanda language">Kinyarwanda</a>: <i lang="rw">u Rwanda</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="rw-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA" title="Help:IPA">[u.ɾɡwaː.nda]</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-2" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/3\/34\/Rwanda_%28rw%29_pronunciation.ogg\/Rwanda_%28rw%29_pronunciation.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;Rwanda (rw) pronunciation.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/3/34/Rwanda_%28rw%29_pronunciation.ogg/Rwanda_%28rw%29_pronunciation.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:Rwanda_(rw)_pronunciation.ogg" title="File:Rwanda (rw) pronunciation.ogg">i</a></sup>), officially the <b>Republic of Rwanda</b>,<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> is a <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked country</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Great_Rift_Valley" title="Great Rift Valley">Great Rift Valley</a> of <a href="/wiki/Central_Africa" title="Central Africa">Central Africa</a>, where the <a href="/wiki/African_Great_Lakes" title="African Great Lakes">African Great Lakes</a> region and <a href="/wiki/Southeast_Africa" title="Southeast Africa">Southeast Africa</a> converge. Located a few degrees south of the <a href="/wiki/Equator" title="Equator">Equator</a>, Rwanda is bordered by <a href="/wiki/Uganda" title="Uganda">Uganda</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tanzania" title="Tanzania">Tanzania</a>, <a href="/wiki/Burundi" title="Burundi">Burundi</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo" title="Democratic Republic of the Congo">Democratic Republic of the Congo</a>. It is highly elevated, giving it the <a href="/wiki/Soubriquet" class="mw-redirect" title="Soubriquet">soubriquet</a> "land of a thousand hills", with its geography dominated by mountains in the west and <a href="/wiki/Savanna" title="Savanna">savanna</a> to the southeast, with numerous lakes throughout the country. The climate is temperate to subtropical, with two rainy seasons and two dry seasons each year. It is the most densely populated mainland African country; among countries larger than 10,000&#160;km<sup>2</sup>, it is the fifth most densely populated country in the world. Its <a href="/wiki/Capital_city" title="Capital city">capital</a> and largest city is <a href="/wiki/Kigali" title="Kigali">Kigali</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Hunter-gatherer" title="Hunter-gatherer">Hunter-gatherers</a> settled the territory in the <a href="/wiki/Stone_Age" title="Stone Age">Stone</a> and <a href="/wiki/Iron_Age" title="Iron Age">Iron Ages</a>, followed later by <a href="/wiki/Bantu_peoples" title="Bantu peoples">Bantu peoples</a>. The population coalesced first into <a href="/wiki/Clans_of_Rwanda" class="mw-redirect" title="Clans of Rwanda">clans</a>, and then, into kingdoms. In the 15th century, one kingdom, under King Gihanga, managed to incorporate several of its close neighbor territories establishing the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Rwanda" title="Kingdom of Rwanda">Kingdom of Rwanda</a>. The Kingdom of Rwanda dominated from the mid-eighteenth century, with the Tutsi kings conquering others militarily, centralising power, and enacting anti-Hutu policies. In 1897, <a href="/wiki/German_colonial_empire" title="German colonial empire">Germany</a> colonized Rwanda as part of <a href="/wiki/German_East_Africa" title="German East Africa">German East Africa</a>, followed by <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgium</a>, which took control in 1916 during <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>. Both European nations ruled through the Rwandan king and perpetuated a pro-Tutsi policy. The Hutu population <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_Revolution" title="Rwandan Revolution">revolted</a> in 1959. They massacred numerous Tutsi and ultimately established an independent, Hutu-dominated republic in 1962 led by President <a href="/wiki/Gr%C3%A9goire_Kayibanda" title="Grégoire Kayibanda">Grégoire Kayibanda</a>. A <a href="/wiki/1973_Rwandan_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" title="1973 Rwandan coup d&#39;état">1973 military coup</a> overthrew Kayibanda and brought <a href="/wiki/Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana" title="Juvénal Habyarimana">Juvénal Habyarimana</a> to power, who retained the pro-Hutu policy. The Tutsi-led <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_Patriotic_Front" title="Rwandan Patriotic Front">Rwandan Patriotic Front</a> (RPF) launched a <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_Civil_War" title="Rwandan Civil War">civil war</a> in 1990. Habyarimana was <a href="/wiki/Assassination_of_Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_and_Cyprien_Ntaryamira" title="Assassination of Juvénal Habyarimana and Cyprien Ntaryamira">assassinated</a> in April 1994. Social tensions erupted in the <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_genocide" title="Rwandan genocide">Rwandan genocide</a> that spanned one hundred days. The RPF ended the genocide with a military victory in July 1994.
</p><p>Rwanda has been governed as <i>de facto</i> <a href="/wiki/One-party_state" title="One-party state">one-party state</a> by the RPF since 1994 with former commander <a href="/wiki/Paul_Kagame" title="Paul Kagame">Paul Kagame</a> as President since 2000. The country has been governed by a series of centralized <a href="/wiki/Authoritarianism" title="Authoritarianism">authoritarian governments</a> since precolonial times. Although Rwanda has low levels of corruption compared with neighbouring countries, it ranks among the lowest in international measurements of government transparency, civil liberties and quality of life. The population is <a href="/wiki/Youth_in_Rwanda" title="Youth in Rwanda">young</a> and predominantly rural; Rwanda has one of the youngest populations in the world. Rwandans are drawn from just one cultural and linguistic group, the <a href="/wiki/Banyarwanda" title="Banyarwanda">Banyarwanda</a>. However, within this group there are three subgroups: the <a href="/wiki/Hutu" title="Hutu">Hutu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tutsi" title="Tutsi">Tutsi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Great_Lakes_Twa" title="Great Lakes Twa">Twa</a>. The Twa are a forest-dwelling <a href="/wiki/Pygmy_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Pygmy people">pygmy people</a> and are often considered descendants of Rwanda's earliest inhabitants. Christianity is the largest religion in the country; the principal and national language is <a href="/wiki/Kinyarwanda" title="Kinyarwanda">Kinyarwanda</a>, spoken by native Rwandans, with English, French and Swahili serving as additional official foreign languages.
</p><p>Rwanda's economy is based mostly on <a href="/wiki/Subsistence_agriculture" title="Subsistence agriculture">subsistence agriculture</a>. <a href="/wiki/Coffee" title="Coffee">Coffee</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tea" title="Tea">tea</a> are the major <a href="/wiki/Cash_crop" title="Cash crop">cash crops</a> in Rwanda to export. <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Rwanda" title="Tourism in Rwanda">Tourism</a> is a fast-growing sector and is now the country's leading foreign exchange earner. The country is a member of the <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union">African Union</a>, the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>, <a href="/wiki/Common_Market_for_Eastern_and_Southern_Africa" title="Common Market for Eastern and Southern Africa">COMESA</a>, <a href="/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie" title="Organisation internationale de la Francophonie">OIF</a> and the <a href="/wiki/East_African_Community" title="East African Community">East African Community</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Rwanda" title="History of Rwanda">History of Rwanda</a>, <a href="/wiki/German_East_Africa" title="German East Africa">German East Africa</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Ruanda-Urundi" title="Ruanda-Urundi">Ruanda-Urundi</a></div>
<p>Modern human settlement of what is now Rwanda dates from, at the latest, the <a href="/wiki/Last_glacial_period" class="mw-redirect" title="Last glacial period">last glacial period</a>, either in the <a href="/wiki/Neolithic" title="Neolithic">Neolithic</a> period around 8000 BC, or in the <a href="/wiki/Neolithic_Subpluvial" class="mw-redirect" title="Neolithic Subpluvial">long humid period</a> which followed, up to around 3000 BC.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien200344_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200344-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Archaeological excavations have revealed evidence of sparse settlement by <a href="/wiki/Hunter-gatherer" title="Hunter-gatherer">hunter-gatherers</a> in the late <a href="/wiki/Stone_Age" title="Stone Age">Stone Age</a>, followed by a larger population of early <a href="/wiki/Iron_Age" title="Iron Age">Iron Age</a> settlers, who produced dimpled <a href="/wiki/Pottery" title="Pottery">pottery</a> and iron tools.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDorsey199436_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDorsey199436-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien200345_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200345-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> These early inhabitants were the ancestors of the <a href="/wiki/Great_Lakes_Twa" title="Great Lakes Twa">Twa</a>, aboriginal <a href="/wiki/Pygmy_peoples" title="Pygmy peoples">pygmy</a> hunter-gatherers who remain in Rwanda today.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMamdani200261_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200261-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Between 700 BC and 1500 AD, a number of <a href="/wiki/Bantu_peoples" title="Bantu peoples">Bantu</a> groups migrated into Rwanda, clearing forest land for agriculture.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMamdani200261_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200261-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien200358_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200358-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The forest-dwelling Twa lost much of their habitat and moved to the mountain slopes.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKing200775_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200775-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Historians have several theories regarding the nature of the Bantu migrations; one theory is that the first settlers were <a href="/wiki/Hutu" title="Hutu">Hutu</a>, while the <a href="/wiki/Tutsi" title="Tutsi">Tutsi</a> migrated later to form a distinct racial group, possibly of Nilo-hamitic origin.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier199516_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199516-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> An alternative theory is that the migration was slow and steady, with incoming groups integrating into rather than conquering the existing society.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMamdani200261_17-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200261-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMamdani200258_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200258-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Under this theory, the Hutu and Tutsi distinction arose later and was a class distinction rather than a racial one.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien200369_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200369-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShyaka10–11_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShyaka10–11-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rwanda_Nyanza_Mwami_Palace.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Rwanda_Nyanza_Mwami_Palace.jpg/220px-Rwanda_Nyanza_Mwami_Palace.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Rwanda_Nyanza_Mwami_Palace.jpg/330px-Rwanda_Nyanza_Mwami_Palace.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Rwanda_Nyanza_Mwami_Palace.jpg/440px-Rwanda_Nyanza_Mwami_Palace.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2272" data-file-height="1704" /></a><figcaption>A reconstruction of the ancient King's Palace at <a href="/wiki/Nyanza,_Rwanda" title="Nyanza, Rwanda">Nyanza</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The earliest form of social organisation in the area was the <a href="/wiki/Clans_of_Rwanda" class="mw-redirect" title="Clans of Rwanda">clan</a> (<i>ubwoko</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien200388_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200388-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The clans were not limited to genealogical lineages or geographical area, and most included Hutu, Tutsi, and Twa.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien200388–89_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200388–89-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> From the 15th century, the clans began to merge into kingdoms.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003141_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003141-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> One kingdom, under King Gihanga, managed to incorporate several of its close neighbor territories establishing the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Rwanda" title="Kingdom of Rwanda">Kingdom of Rwanda</a>. By 1700, around eight kingdoms had existed in the present-day Rwanda.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003482_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003482-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> One of these, the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Rwanda" title="Kingdom of Rwanda">Kingdom of Rwanda</a> ruled by the Tutsi <a href="/wiki/Nyiginya" title="Nyiginya">Nyiginya</a> clan, became increasingly dominant from the mid-eighteenth century.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003160_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003160-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The kingdom reached its greatest extent during the nineteenth century under the reign of King <a href="/wiki/Kigeli_IV_Rwabugiri" title="Kigeli IV Rwabugiri">Kigeli Rwabugiri</a>. Rwabugiri conquered several smaller states, expanded the kingdom west and north,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003160_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003160-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMamdani200269_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200269-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> and initiated administrative reforms; these included <span title="Kinyarwanda-language text"><i lang="rw"><a href="/wiki/Ubuhake" title="Ubuhake">ubuhake</a></i></span>, in which Tutsi patrons ceded cattle, and therefore privileged status, to Hutu or Tutsi clients in exchange for economic and personal service,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier199513–14_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199513–14-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> and <span title="Kinyarwanda-language text"><i lang="rw">uburetwa</i></span>, a <a href="/wiki/Corv%C3%A9e" title="Corvée">corvée</a> system in which Hutu were forced to work for Tutsi chiefs.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMamdani200269_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200269-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Rwabugiri's changes caused a rift to grow between the Hutu and Tutsi populations.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMamdani200269_29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200269-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The Twa were better off than in pre-Kingdom days, with some becoming dancers in the royal court,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKing200775_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200775-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> but their numbers continued to decline.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier19956_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier19956-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Berlin_Conference" title="Berlin Conference">Berlin Conference</a> of 1884 assigned the territory to the <a href="/wiki/German_Empire" title="German Empire">German Empire</a>, who declared it as part of the <a href="/wiki/German_East_Africa" title="German East Africa">German East Africa</a>. In 1894, explorer <a href="/wiki/Gustav_Adolf_von_G%C3%B6tzen" title="Gustav Adolf von Götzen">Gustav Adolf von Götzen</a> was the first European to cross the entire territory of Rwanda; he crossed from the south-east to Lake Kivu and met the king.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003217_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003217-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier19959_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier19959-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> In 1897, Germany established a presence in Rwanda with the formation of an alliance with the king, beginning the colonial era.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> The Germans did not significantly alter the social structure of the country, but exerted influence by supporting the king and the existing hierarchy, and delegating power to local chiefs.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier199525_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199525-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Belgian_colonial_empire" title="Belgian colonial empire">Belgian</a> forces invaded Rwanda and <a href="/wiki/Burundi" title="Burundi">Burundi</a> in 1916, during <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, and later, in 1922, they started to rule both Rwanda and Burundi as a <a href="/wiki/League_of_Nations_mandate" title="League of Nations mandate">League of Nations mandate</a> called <a href="/wiki/Ruanda-Urundi" title="Ruanda-Urundi">Ruanda-Urundi</a> and started a period of more direct colonial rule<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003260_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003260-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> The Belgians simplified and centralised the power structure,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003270_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003270-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> introduced large-scale projects in education, health, public works, and agricultural supervision, including new crops and improved agricultural techniques to try to reduce the incidence of famine.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003276–277_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003276–277-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Both the Germans and the Belgians, in the wake of <a href="/wiki/New_Imperialism" title="New Imperialism">New Imperialism</a>, promoted Tutsi supremacy, considering the Hutu and Tutsi different <a href="/wiki/Race_(human_categorization)" title="Race (human categorization)">races</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAppiahGates2010450_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAppiahGates2010450-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> In 1935, Belgium introduced an identity card system, which labelled each individual as either Tutsi, Hutu, Twa or Naturalised. While it had been previously possible for particularly wealthy Hutu to become honorary Tutsi, the identity cards prevented any further movement between the classes.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGourevitch200056–57_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGourevitch200056–57-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_(1980).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Photograph of President Juvénal Habyarimana arriving with entourage at Andrews Air Force Base, Maryland, USA on 25 September 1980." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_%281980%29.jpg/170px-Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_%281980%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="239" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_%281980%29.jpg/255px-Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_%281980%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_%281980%29.jpg/340px-Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_%281980%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="916" data-file-height="1287" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana" title="Juvénal Habyarimana">Juvénal Habyarimana</a>, president from 1973 to 1994</figcaption></figure>
<p>Belgium continued to rule <a href="/wiki/Ruanda-Urundi" title="Ruanda-Urundi">Ruanda-Urundi</a> (of which Rwanda formed the northern part) as a <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_trust_territories" title="United Nations trust territories">UN trust territory</a> after the <a href="/wiki/Second_World_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Second World War">Second World War</a>, with a mandate to oversee eventual <a href="/wiki/Decolonisation_of_Africa" title="Decolonisation of Africa">independence</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_(II)_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_(II)-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_(III)_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_(III)-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> Tensions escalated between the Tutsi, who favoured early independence, and the Hutu emancipation movement, culminating in the 1959 <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_Revolution" title="Rwandan Revolution">Rwandan Revolution</a>: Hutu activists began killing Tutsi and destroying their houses,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELindenLinden1977267_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELindenLinden1977267-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> forcing more than 100,000 people to seek refuge in neighbouring countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGourevitch200058–59_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGourevitch200058–59-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier199551_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199551-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> In 1961, the suddenly pro-Hutu Belgians held <a href="/wiki/1961_Rwandan_monarchy_referendum" title="1961 Rwandan monarchy referendum">a referendum</a> in which the country voted to abolish the monarchy. Rwanda was separated from Burundi and gained independence on 1 July 1962,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier199553_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199553-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> which is commemorated as Independence Day, a national holiday.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Cycles of violence followed, with exiled Tutsi attacking from neighbouring countries and the Hutu retaliating with large-scale slaughter and repression of the Tutsi.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier199556_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199556-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> In 1973, <a href="/wiki/Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana" title="Juvénal Habyarimana">Juvénal Habyarimana</a> took power in <a href="/wiki/1973_Rwandan_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" title="1973 Rwandan coup d&#39;état">a military coup</a>. Pro-Hutu discrimination continued, but there was greater economic prosperity and a reduced amount of violence against Tutsi.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier199574–76_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199574–76-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> The Twa remained marginalised, and by 1990 were almost entirely forced out of the forests by the government; many became beggars.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUNPO2008History_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNPO2008History-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda's population had increased from 1.6&#160;million people in 1934 to 7.1&#160;million in 1989, leading to competition for land.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier19954_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier19954-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Nyamata_Memorial_Site_13.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Nyamata_Memorial_Site_13.jpg/220px-Nyamata_Memorial_Site_13.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Nyamata_Memorial_Site_13.jpg/330px-Nyamata_Memorial_Site_13.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Nyamata_Memorial_Site_13.jpg/440px-Nyamata_Memorial_Site_13.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3008" data-file-height="2000" /></a><figcaption>Human skulls at the <a href="/wiki/Nyamata" title="Nyamata">Nyamata</a> Genocide Memorial</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1990, the <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_Patriotic_Front" title="Rwandan Patriotic Front">Rwandan Patriotic Front</a> (RPF), a rebel group composed of Tutsi refugees, invaded northern Rwanda from their base in Uganda, initiating the <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_Civil_War" title="Rwandan Civil War">Rwandan Civil War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier199593_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199593-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> The group condemned the Hutu-dominated government for failing to democratize and confront the problems facing these refugees. Neither side was able to gain a decisive advantage in the war,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier1995135–136_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier1995135–136-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> but by 1992 it had weakened Habyarimana's authority; mass demonstrations forced him into a coalition with the domestic opposition and eventually to sign the 1993 <a href="/wiki/Arusha_Accords_(Rwanda)" title="Arusha Accords (Rwanda)">Arusha Accords</a> with the RPF.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier1995190–191_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier1995190–191-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> The cease-fire ended on 6 April 1994 when <a href="/wiki/Assassination_of_Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_and_Cyprien_Ntaryamira" title="Assassination of Juvénal Habyarimana and Cyprien Ntaryamira">Habyarimana's plane was shot down</a> near Kigali Airport, killing him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(III)2010_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(III)2010-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> The shooting down of the plane served as the catalyst for the <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_genocide" title="Rwandan genocide">Rwandan genocide</a>, which began within a few hours. Over the course of approximately 100 days, between 500,000 and 1,000,000<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHenley2007_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHenley2007-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Tutsi and politically moderate Hutu were killed in well-planned attacks on the orders of the interim government.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDallaire2005386_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDallaire2005386-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Many Twa were also killed, despite not being directly targeted.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUNPO2008History_51-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNPO2008History-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Tutsi RPF restarted their offensive, and took control of the country methodically, gaining control of the whole country by mid-July.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDallaire2005299_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDallaire2005299-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> The international response to the genocide was limited, with major powers reluctant to strengthen the already overstretched <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Assistance_Mission_for_Rwanda" title="United Nations Assistance Mission for Rwanda">UN peacekeeping force</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDallaire2005364_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDallaire2005364-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> When the RPF took over, approximately two million Hutu <a href="/wiki/Great_Lakes_refugee_crisis" title="Great Lakes refugee crisis">fled to neighbouring countries</a>, in particular <a href="/wiki/Za%C3%AFre" class="mw-redirect" title="Zaïre">Zaïre</a>, fearing reprisals;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier1995312_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier1995312-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> additionally, the RPF-led army was a key belligerent in the <a href="/wiki/First_Congo_War" title="First Congo War">First</a> and <a href="/wiki/Second_Congo_War" title="Second Congo War">Second Congo Wars</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(V)2010_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(V)2010-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> Within Rwanda, a period of reconciliation and justice began, with the establishment of the <a href="/wiki/International_Criminal_Tribunal_for_Rwanda" title="International Criminal Tribunal for Rwanda">International Criminal Tribunal for Rwanda</a> (ICTR) and the reintroduction of <span title="Kinyarwanda-language text"><i lang="rw"><a href="/wiki/Gacaca" class="mw-redirect" title="Gacaca">Gacaca</a></i></span>, a traditional village court system.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBowcott2014_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBowcott2014-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Since 2000 Rwanda's economy,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(X)_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(X)-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> tourist numbers,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(XI)_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(XI)-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a> have grown rapidly;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(I)2010_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(I)2010-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> between 2006 and 2011 the poverty rate reduced from 57% to 45%,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda2012_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda2012-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> while <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">life expectancy</a> rose from 46.6 years in 2000<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(V)20132_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(V)20132-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> to 65.4 years in 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics_and_government">Politics and government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Politics and government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Rwanda" title="Politics of Rwanda">Politics of Rwanda</a>, <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Rwanda" title="Foreign relations of Rwanda">Foreign relations of Rwanda</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Rwanda_Defence_Force" title="Rwanda Defence Force">Rwanda Defence Force</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paul_Kagame_2014.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Photograph of Paul Kagame, taken in Busan, South Korea, in 2014" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Paul_Kagame_2014.jpg/170px-Paul_Kagame_2014.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="225" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Paul_Kagame_2014.jpg/255px-Paul_Kagame_2014.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Paul_Kagame_2014.jpg/340px-Paul_Kagame_2014.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1909" data-file-height="2531" /></a><figcaption>Rwandan President <a href="/wiki/Paul_Kagame" title="Paul Kagame">Paul Kagame</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/President_of_Rwanda" class="mw-redirect" title="President of Rwanda">president of Rwanda</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_98_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_98-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> and has broad powers including creating policy in conjunction with the <a href="/wiki/Cabinet_of_Rwanda" title="Cabinet of Rwanda">Cabinet of Rwanda</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_117_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_117-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> exercising the <a href="/wiki/Prerogative_of_mercy_(Rwanda)" class="mw-redirect" title="Prerogative of mercy (Rwanda)">prerogative of mercy</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_111_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_111-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> commanding the <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_Defence_Forces" class="mw-redirect" title="Rwandan Defence Forces">armed forces</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_110_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_110-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> negotiating and ratifying treaties,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_189_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_189-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> signing presidential orders,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_112_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_112-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> and declaring war or a state of emergency.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_110_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_110-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> The president is elected by popular vote <a href="/wiki/Elections_in_Rwanda" title="Elections in Rwanda">every seven years</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003articles_100–101_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003articles_100–101-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> and appoints the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Rwanda" title="Prime Minister of Rwanda">prime minister</a> and all other members of the Cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_116_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_116-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> The incumbent president is <a href="/wiki/Paul_Kagame" title="Paul Kagame">Paul Kagame</a>, who took office upon the resignation of his predecessor, <a href="/wiki/Pasteur_Bizimungu" title="Pasteur Bizimungu">Pasteur Bizimungu</a>, in 2000. Kagame subsequently won elections in <a href="/wiki/2003_Rwandan_presidential_election" title="2003 Rwandan presidential election">2003</a> and <a href="/wiki/2010_Rwandan_presidential_election" title="2010 Rwandan presidential election">2010</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELacey2003_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELacey2003-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(IV)2010_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(IV)2010-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Although human rights organisations have criticised these elections as being "marked by increasing political repression and a crackdown on free speech".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHuman_Rights_Watch2010_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuman_Rights_Watch2010-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> Article 101 of the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Rwanda" title="Constitution of Rwanda">constitution</a> had previously limited presidents to two terms in office,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMufulukye2015_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMufulukye2015-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> but this was changed in a <a href="/wiki/2015_Rwandan_constitutional_referendum" title="2015 Rwandan constitutional referendum">2015 referendum</a>, which had been brought following receipt of a petition signed by 3.8&#160;million Rwandans.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUwiringiyamana_(I)2015_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUwiringiyamana_(I)2015-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Through this change in the constitution, Kagame could stay on as president until 2034.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUwiringiyamana_(II)2015_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUwiringiyamana_(II)2015-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Kagame was elected for a third term in <a href="/wiki/2017_Rwandan_presidential_election" title="2017 Rwandan presidential election">2017</a> with 98.79% of the vote.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBurke2017_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurke2017-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHuman_Rights_Watch2017_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuman_Rights_Watch2017-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The constitution was adopted following a national referendum in 2003, replacing the transitional constitution which had been in place since 1994.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPanapress2003_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPanapress2003-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> The constitution mandates a multi-party system of government, with politics based on democracy and elections.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_52_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_52-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> However, the constitution places conditions on how political parties may operate. Article 54 states that "political organizations are prohibited from basing themselves on race, ethnic group, tribe, clan, region, sex, religion or any other division which may give rise to discrimination".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_54_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_54-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> The government has also enacted laws criminalising genocide ideology, which can include intimidation, defamatory speeches, <a href="/wiki/Genocide_denial" title="Genocide denial">genocide denial</a> and mocking of victims.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENational_Commission_for_the_Fight_against_Genocide20081_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Commission_for_the_Fight_against_Genocide20081-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch" title="Human Rights Watch">Human Rights Watch</a>, these laws effectively make Rwanda a one-party state, as "under the guise of preventing another genocide, the government displays a marked intolerance of the most basic forms of dissent".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoth2009_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoth2009-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Amnesty_International" title="Amnesty International">Amnesty International</a> is also critical; in its 2014/15 report, Amnesty said that laws against inciting insurrection or trouble among the population had been used to imprison people "for the legitimate exercise of their rights to freedom of association or of expression".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International2015311_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International2015311-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Rwanda" title="Parliament of Rwanda">Parliament</a> consists of two <a href="/wiki/Legislative_chamber" title="Legislative chamber">chambers</a>. It makes legislation and is empowered by the constitution to oversee the activities of the president and the Cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_62_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_62-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> The lower chamber is the <a href="/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_(Rwanda)" title="Chamber of Deputies (Rwanda)">Chamber of Deputies</a>, which has 80 members serving five-year terms. Twenty-four of these seats are reserved for women, elected through a joint assembly of local government officials; another three seats are reserved for youth and disabled members; the remaining 53 are elected by <a href="/wiki/Universal_suffrage" title="Universal suffrage">universal suffrage</a> under a <a href="/wiki/Proportional_representation" title="Proportional representation">proportional representation</a> system.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_76_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_76-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> Following the <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_parliamentary_election,_2018" class="mw-redirect" title="Rwandan parliamentary election, 2018">2018 election</a>, there are 49 female deputies,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKwibuka2018_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKwibuka2018-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> down from 51 in 2013;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMunyaneza2013_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMunyaneza2013-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> as of 2020<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, Rwanda is one of only three countries with a female majority in the national parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEInter-Parliamentary_Union2020_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEInter-Parliamentary_Union2020-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> The upper chamber is the 26-seat <a href="/wiki/Senate_(Rwanda)" title="Senate (Rwanda)">Senate</a>, whose members are selected by a variety of bodies. A mandatory minimum of 30% of the senators are women. Senators serve eight-year terms.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_82_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_82-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> (See also <a href="/wiki/Gender_equality_in_Rwanda" title="Gender equality in Rwanda">Gender equality in Rwanda</a>.)
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:RwandaParliament.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Photograph of the Chamber of Deputies with highway in the foreground" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/RwandaParliament.jpg/220px-RwandaParliament.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/RwandaParliament.jpg/330px-RwandaParliament.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/RwandaParliament.jpg/440px-RwandaParliament.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_of_Rwanda" class="mw-redirect" title="Chamber of Deputies of Rwanda">Chamber of Deputies building</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Rwanda's legal system is largely based on <a href="/wiki/Law_of_Germany" title="Law of Germany">German</a> and <a href="/wiki/Law_of_Belgium" title="Law of Belgium">Belgian</a> <a href="/wiki/Civil_law_(legal_system)" title="Civil law (legal system)">civil law</a> systems and <a href="/wiki/Customary_law" title="Customary law">customary law</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The judiciary is independent of the executive branch,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_140_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_140-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> although the president and the Senate are involved in the appointment of Supreme Court judges.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_148_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_148-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> Human Rights Watch have praised the Rwandan government for progress made in the delivery of justice including the abolition of the death penalty,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHuman_Rights_WatchWells2008I._Summary_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuman_Rights_WatchWells2008I._Summary-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> but also allege interference in the judicial system by members of the government, such as the politically motivated appointment of judges, misuse of prosecutorial power, and pressure on judges to make particular decisions.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHuman_Rights_WatchWells2008VIII._Independence_of_the_Judiciary_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuman_Rights_WatchWells2008VIII._Independence_of_the_Judiciary-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> The constitution provides for two types of courts: ordinary and specialised.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_143_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_143-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> Ordinary courts are the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Rwanda" title="Supreme Court of Rwanda">Supreme Court</a>, the <a href="/w/index.php?title=High_Court_of_Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="High Court of Rwanda (page does not exist)">High Court</a>, and regional courts, while specialised courts are military courts<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_143_102-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_143-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> and a system of commercial courts created in 2011 to expedite commercial litigations.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKamere2011_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKamere2011-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> Between 2004 and 2012, a system of <a href="/wiki/Gacaca_court" title="Gacaca court"><i>Gacaca</i> courts</a> was in operation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VIII)2015_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VIII)2015-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> <span title="Kinyarwanda-language text"><i lang="rw">Gacaca</i></span>, a Rwandan traditional court operated by villages and communities, was revived to expedite the trials of genocide suspects.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWalkerMarch_2004_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalkerMarch_2004-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> The court succeeded in clearing the backlog of genocide cases, but was criticised by human rights groups as not meeting legal fair standard.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(IX)2012_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(IX)2012-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rwanda has low corruption levels relative to most other African countries; in 2014, <a href="/wiki/Transparency_International" title="Transparency International">Transparency International</a> ranked Rwanda as the fifth cleanest out of 47 countries in <a href="/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africa" title="Sub-Saharan Africa">Sub-Saharan Africa</a> and 55th cleanest out of 175 in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETransparency_International2014_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETransparency_International2014-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAgutamba2014_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgutamba2014-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> The constitution provides for an <a href="/wiki/Ombudsman" title="Ombudsman">ombudsman</a>, whose duties include prevention and fighting of corruption.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_182_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_182-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOffice_of_the_Ombudsman_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOffice_of_the_Ombudsman-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Public officials (including the president) are required by the constitution to declare their wealth to the ombudsman and to the public; those who do not comply are suspended from office.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAsiimwe2011_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAsiimwe2011-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Despite this, Human Rights Watch notes extensive political repression throughout the country, including illegal and arbitrary detention, threats or other forms of intimidation, disappearances, politically motivated trials, and the massacre of peacefully protesting civilians.<sup id="cite_ref-Roth_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Roth-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_Patriotic_Front" title="Rwandan Patriotic Front">Rwandan Patriotic Front</a> (RPF) has been the dominant <a href="/wiki/Political_party" title="Political party">political party</a> in the country since 1994. The RPF has maintained control of the presidency and the Parliament in national elections, with the party's vote share consistently exceeding 70%. The RPF is seen as a Tutsi-dominated party but receives support from across the country, and is credited with ensuring continued peace, stability, and economic growth.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEClark2010_113-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEClark2010-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> According to human rights organisation <a href="/wiki/Freedom_House" title="Freedom House">Freedom House</a> 2015 report the government has been suppressing the freedoms of opposition groups in that the RPF had "prevented new political parties from registering and arrested the leaders of several existing parties, effectively preventing them from fielding candidates" in elections.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFreedom_House2015_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreedom_House2015-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> Amnesty International also claims that the RPF rules Rwanda "without any meaningful opposition".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International2015310_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International2015310-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rwanda is a member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_(I)_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_(I)-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union">African Union</a>, <a href="/wiki/La_Francophonie" class="mw-redirect" title="La Francophonie">Francophonie</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrancophonie_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrancophonie-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/East_African_Community" title="East African Community">East African Community</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGrainger2007_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrainger2007-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFletcher2009_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFletcher2009-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> For many years during the Habyarimana regime, the country maintained close ties with France, as well as Belgium, the former colonial power.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier199589_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199589-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Under the RPF government, however, Rwanda has sought closer ties with neighbouring countries in the East African Community and with the English-speaking world. Diplomatic relations with France were suspended in 2006 following the indictment of Rwandan officials by a French judge,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPorter2008_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPorter2008-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> and despite their restoration in 2010, as of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> relations between the countries remain strained.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEXinhua_News_Agency2015_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEXinhua_News_Agency2015-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> Relations with the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo" title="Democratic Republic of the Congo">Democratic Republic of the Congo</a> were tense following Rwanda's involvement in the <a href="/wiki/First_Congo_War" title="First Congo War">First</a> and <a href="/wiki/Second_Congo_War" title="Second Congo War">Second Congo Wars</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(V)2010_62-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(V)2010-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> the Congolese army alleged Rwandan attacks on their troops, while Rwanda blamed the Congolese government for failing to suppress Hutu rebels in <a href="/wiki/North_Kivu" title="North Kivu">North</a> and <a href="/wiki/South_Kivu" title="South Kivu">South Kivu</a> provinces.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUSA_Today2008_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSA_Today2008-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAl_Jazeera2007_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl_Jazeera2007-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> In 2010, the United Nations released a report accusing the Rwandan army of committing wide scale human rights violations and crimes against humanity in the Democratic Republic of the Congo during the <a href="/wiki/First_Congo_War" title="First Congo War">First</a> and <a href="/wiki/Second_Congo_War" title="Second Congo War">Second Congo Wars</a>, charges denied by the Rwandan government.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMcGreal2010_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGreal2010-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> Relations soured further in 2012, as Kinshasa accused Rwanda of supporting the <a href="/wiki/M23_rebellion" title="M23 rebellion">M23 rebellion</a>, an insurgency in the eastern Congo.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(X)2012_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(X)2012-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> As of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, peace has been restored and relations are improving.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAgence_Africaine_de_Presse2015_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgence_Africaine_de_Presse2015-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda's relationship with <a href="/wiki/Uganda" title="Uganda">Uganda</a> was also tense for much of the 2000s following a 1999 clash between the two countries' armies as they backed opposing rebel groups in the Second Congo War,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHeuler2011_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeuler2011-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> but improved significantly in the early 2010s.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VI)2011_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VI)2011-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMaboja2015_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaboja2015-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> In 2019, relations between the two countries deteriorated, with Rwanda closing its borders with Uganda.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Decentralization_in_Rwanda" title="Decentralization in Rwanda">Decentralization in Rwanda</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:RwandaGeoProvinces.png" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Map of Rwanda showing the five provinces in various colours, as well as major cities, lakes, rivers, and areas of neighbouring countries" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e5/RwandaGeoProvinces.png/220px-RwandaGeoProvinces.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="213" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/e5/RwandaGeoProvinces.png 1.5x" data-file-width="327" data-file-height="317" /></a><figcaption>Provinces of Rwanda</figcaption></figure>
<p>Before western colonization, the Rwandan government system had a quasi-system of political pluralism and power sharing. <sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOAU200014_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOAU200014-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Despite there being a strict hierarchy, the pre-colonial system achieved an established, combined system of "centralized power and decentralized autonomous units." Under the monarch, the elected Chief governed a province that was divided into multiple districts. Two other officials appointed by head Chief governed the districts; one official was allocated power over the land while the other oversaw cattle. The <a href="/wiki/List_of_kings_of_Rwanda" title="List of kings of Rwanda">king</a> (<span title="Kinyarwanda-language text"><i lang="rw">mwami</i></span>) exercised control through a system of provinces, districts, hills, and neighbourhoods.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMelvern20045_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMelvern20045-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
As of 2003, the constitution <a href="/wiki/Subdivisions_of_Rwanda" title="Subdivisions of Rwanda">divided</a> Rwanda into <a href="/wiki/Provinces_of_Rwanda" title="Provinces of Rwanda">provinces</a> (<span title="Kinyarwanda-language text"><i lang="rw">intara</i></span>), <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Rwanda" title="Districts of Rwanda">districts</a> (<span title="Kinyarwanda-language text"><i lang="rw">uturere</i></span>), cities, municipalities, towns, <a href="/wiki/Sectors_of_Rwanda" title="Sectors of Rwanda">sectors</a> (<span title="Kinyarwanda-language text"><i lang="rw">imirenge</i></span>), cells (<span title="Kinyarwanda-language text"><i lang="rw">utugari</i></span>), and villages (<span title="Kinyarwanda-language text"><i lang="rw">imidugudu</i></span>); the larger divisions, and their borders, are established by Parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_3_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_3-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> In January 2006, Rwanda was reorganized such that twelve provinces were merged to create five, and 106 districts were merged into thirty.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> The present borders drawn in 2006 aimed át decentralising power and removing associations with the old system and the genocide. The previous structure of twelve provinces associated with the largest cities was replaced with five provinces based primarily on geography.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(I)2006_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(I)2006-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> These are <a href="/wiki/Northern_Province,_Rwanda" title="Northern Province, Rwanda">Northern Province</a>, <a href="/wiki/Southern_Province,_Rwanda" title="Southern Province, Rwanda">Southern Province</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Province,_Rwanda" title="Eastern Province, Rwanda">Eastern Province</a>, <a href="/wiki/Western_Province,_Rwanda" title="Western Province, Rwanda">Western Province</a>, and the Municipality of Kigali in the centre.
</p><p>The five provinces act as intermediaries between the national government and their constituent districts to ensure that national policies are implemented at the district level. The Rwanda Decentralisation Strategic Framework developed by the Ministry of Local Government assigns to provinces the responsibility for "coordinating governance issues in the Province, as well as monitoring and evaluation".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20078_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20078-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Each province is headed by a governor, appointed by the president and approved by the Senate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESouthern_Province_139-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern_Province-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> The districts are responsible for coordinating public service delivery and economic development. They are divided into sectors, which are responsible for the delivery of public services as mandated by the districts.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20079_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20079-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> Districts and sectors have directly elected councils, and are run by an executive committee selected by that council.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINALOC2004_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC2004-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> The cells and villages are the smallest political units, providing a link between the people and the sectors.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20079_140-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20079-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> All adult resident citizens are members of their local cell council, from which an executive committee is elected.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINALOC2004_141-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC2004-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> The city of <a href="/wiki/Kigali" title="Kigali">Kigali</a> is a provincial-level authority, which coordinates urban planning within the city.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20078_138-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20078-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Rwanda" title="Geography of Rwanda">Geography of Rwanda</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rwanda_Topography.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/11/Rwanda_Topography.png/290px-Rwanda_Topography.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="228" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/11/Rwanda_Topography.png/435px-Rwanda_Topography.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/11/Rwanda_Topography.png/580px-Rwanda_Topography.png 2x" data-file-width="1631" data-file-height="1280" /></a><figcaption>Topography of Rwanda</figcaption></figure>
<p>At 26,338 square kilometres (10,169&#160;sq&#160;mi), Rwanda is the world's 149th-largest country,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(II)_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(II)-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> and the fourth smallest on the African mainland after <a href="/wiki/Gambia" class="mw-redirect" title="Gambia">Gambia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eswatini" title="Eswatini">Eswatini</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Djibouti" title="Djibouti">Djibouti</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(II)_142-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(II)-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> It is comparable in size to <a href="/wiki/Burundi" title="Burundi">Burundi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Haiti" title="Haiti">Haiti</a> and <a href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichards1994_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichards1994-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> The entire country is at a high altitude: the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_lowest_point" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by lowest point">lowest point</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Rusizi_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Rusizi River">Rusizi River</a> at 950 metres (3,117&#160;ft) above sea level.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda is located in Central/Eastern Africa, and is bordered by the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo" title="Democratic Republic of the Congo">Democratic Republic of the Congo</a> to the west, <a href="/wiki/Uganda" title="Uganda">Uganda</a> to the north, <a href="/wiki/Tanzania" title="Tanzania">Tanzania</a> to the east, and <a href="/wiki/Burundi" title="Burundi">Burundi</a> to the south.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> It lies a few degrees south of the <a href="/wiki/Equator" title="Equator">equator</a> and is <a href="/wiki/Landlocked" class="mw-redirect" title="Landlocked">landlocked</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2004_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2004-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> The capital, Kigali, is located near the centre of Rwanda.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEncyclopædia_Britannica2010_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEncyclopædia_Britannica2010-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Congo-Nile_Divide" title="Congo-Nile Divide">watershed</a> between the major <a href="/wiki/Congo_Basin" title="Congo Basin">Congo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nile_Basin" title="Nile Basin">Nile</a> <a href="/wiki/Drainage_basin" title="Drainage basin">drainage basins</a> runs from north to south through Rwanda, with around 80% of the country's area draining into the Nile and 20% into the Congo via the <a href="/wiki/Rusizi_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Rusizi River">Rusizi River</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lake_Tanganyika" title="Lake Tanganyika">Lake Tanganyika</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENile_Basin_Initiative2010_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENile_Basin_Initiative2010-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The country's longest river is the <a href="/wiki/Nyabarongo_River_Wetlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Nyabarongo River Wetlands">Nyabarongo</a>, which rises in the south-west, flows north, east, and southeast before merging with the <a href="/wiki/Ruvubu_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Ruvubu River">Ruvubu</a> to form the <a href="/wiki/Kagera_River" title="Kagera River">Kagera</a>; the Kagera then flows due north along the eastern border with Tanzania. The Nyabarongo-Kagera eventually drains into <a href="/wiki/Lake_Victoria" title="Lake Victoria">Lake Victoria</a>, and its source in Nyungwe Forest is a contender for the as-yet undetermined overall <a href="/wiki/Source_(river_or_stream)" class="mw-redirect" title="Source (river or stream)">source</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Nile" title="Nile">Nile</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(II)2006_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(II)2006-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda has many lakes, the largest being <a href="/wiki/Lake_Kivu" title="Lake Kivu">Lake Kivu</a>. This lake occupies the floor of the <a href="/wiki/Albertine_Rift" title="Albertine Rift">Albertine Rift</a> along most of the length of Rwanda's western border, and with a maximum depth of 480 metres (1,575&#160;ft),<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJørgensen200593_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJørgensen200593-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> it is one of the twenty <a href="/wiki/List_of_lakes_by_depth" title="List of lakes by depth">deepest lakes in the world</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth2006153_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth2006153-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> Other sizeable lakes include <a href="/wiki/Lake_Burera" title="Lake Burera">Burera</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lake_Ruhondo" title="Lake Ruhondo">Ruhondo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lake_Muhazi" title="Lake Muhazi">Muhazi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lake_Rweru" title="Lake Rweru">Rweru</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Lake_Ihema" title="Lake Ihema">Ihema</a>, the last being the largest of a string of lakes in the eastern plains of <a href="/wiki/Akagera_National_Park" title="Akagera National Park">Akagera National Park</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHodd1994522_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHodd1994522-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mountains dominate central and western Rwanda and the country is sometimes called "<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Pays des mille collines</i></span>" in French ("Land of a thousand hills").<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> They are part of the Albertine Rift Mountains that flank the Albertine branch of the <a href="/wiki/East_African_Rift" title="East African Rift">East African Rift</a>, which runs from north to south along Rwanda's western border.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWWF2001Location_and_General_Description_152-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWWF2001Location_and_General_Description-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> The highest peaks are found in the <a href="/wiki/Virunga_Mountains" title="Virunga Mountains">Virunga</a> volcano chain in the northwest; this includes <a href="/wiki/Mount_Karisimbi" title="Mount Karisimbi">Mount Karisimbi</a>, Rwanda's highest point, at 4,507 metres (14,787&#160;ft).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMehtaKatee200537_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMehtaKatee200537-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> This western section of the country lies within the <a href="/wiki/Albertine_Rift_montane_forests" title="Albertine Rift montane forests">Albertine Rift montane forests</a> ecoregion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWWF2001Location_and_General_Description_152-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWWF2001Location_and_General_Description-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> It has an elevation of 1,500 to 2,500 metres (4,921 to 8,202&#160;ft).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMunyakaziNtagaramba20057_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMunyakaziNtagaramba20057-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> The centre of the country is predominantly rolling hills, while the eastern border region consists of <a href="/wiki/Savanna" title="Savanna">savanna</a>, plains and swamps.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMunyakaziNtagaramba200518_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMunyakaziNtagaramba200518-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:RwandaVolcanoAndLake_cropped2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Photograph of a lake with one of the Virunga mountains behind, partially in cloud" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/RwandaVolcanoAndLake_cropped2.jpg/220px-RwandaVolcanoAndLake_cropped2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/RwandaVolcanoAndLake_cropped2.jpg/330px-RwandaVolcanoAndLake_cropped2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/RwandaVolcanoAndLake_cropped2.jpg/440px-RwandaVolcanoAndLake_cropped2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a><figcaption>Lake and volcano in the <a href="/wiki/Virunga_Mountains" title="Virunga Mountains">Virunga Mountains</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Rwanda has a <a href="/wiki/Temperate" class="mw-redirect" title="Temperate">temperate</a> <a href="/wiki/Tropical_climate" title="Tropical climate">tropical highland</a> climate, with lower temperatures than are typical for equatorial countries because of its high elevation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2004_144-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2004-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Kigali, in the centre of the country, has a typical daily temperature range between 15 and 28&#160;°C (59 and 82&#160;°F), with little variation through the year.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Meteorological_Organization_156-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Meteorological_Organization-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> There are some temperature variations across the country; the mountainous west and north are generally cooler than the lower-lying east.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBest_Country_Reports2007_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBest_Country_Reports2007-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> There are two rainy seasons in the year; the first runs from February to June and the second from September to December. These are separated by two <a href="/wiki/Dry_season" title="Dry season">dry seasons</a>: the major one from June to September, during which there is often no rain at all, and a shorter and less severe one from December to February.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKing200710_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200710-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> Rainfall varies geographically, with the west and northwest of the country receiving more precipitation annually than the east and southeast.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle20071_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle20071-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Global_warming" class="mw-redirect" title="Global warming">Global warming</a> has caused a change in the pattern of the rainy seasons. According to a report by the Strategic Foresight Group, change in climate has reduced the number of rainy days experienced during a year, but has also caused an increase in frequency of torrential rains.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStrategic_Foresight_Group201329_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStrategic_Foresight_Group201329-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Both changes have caused difficulty for farmers, decreasing their productivity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBucyensenge2014_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBucyensenge2014-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> Strategic Foresight also characterise Rwanda as a fast warming country, with an increase in average temperature of between 0.7&#160;°C to 0.9&#160;°C over fifty years.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStrategic_Foresight_Group201329_160-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStrategic_Foresight_Group201329-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity">Biodiversity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Biodiversity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_of_Rwanda" title="Wildlife of Rwanda">Wildlife of Rwanda</a></div>
