<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mountain_gorilla_from_Susa_Group_in_Karisimbi_thicket_of_Volcanoes_National_Park_in_Rwanda._Emmanuel_Kwizera.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/Mountain_gorilla_from_Susa_Group_in_Karisimbi_thicket_of_Volcanoes_National_Park_in_Rwanda._Emmanuel_Kwizera.jpg/220px-Mountain_gorilla_from_Susa_Group_in_Karisimbi_thicket_of_Volcanoes_National_Park_in_Rwanda._Emmanuel_Kwizera.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/Mountain_gorilla_from_Susa_Group_in_Karisimbi_thicket_of_Volcanoes_National_Park_in_Rwanda._Emmanuel_Kwizera.jpg/330px-Mountain_gorilla_from_Susa_Group_in_Karisimbi_thicket_of_Volcanoes_National_Park_in_Rwanda._Emmanuel_Kwizera.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/Mountain_gorilla_from_Susa_Group_in_Karisimbi_thicket_of_Volcanoes_National_Park_in_Rwanda._Emmanuel_Kwizera.jpg/440px-Mountain_gorilla_from_Susa_Group_in_Karisimbi_thicket_of_Volcanoes_National_Park_in_Rwanda._Emmanuel_Kwizera.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6720" data-file-height="4480" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Volcanoes_National_Park" title="Volcanoes National Park">Volcanoes National Park</a> is the home of the largest population of <a href="/wiki/Mountain_Gorillas" class="mw-redirect" title="Mountain Gorillas">Mountain Gorillas</a> in the world.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In prehistoric times <a href="/wiki/Montane_forest" class="mw-redirect" title="Montane forest">montane forest</a> occupied one-third of the territory of present-day Rwanda. Naturally occurring vegetation is now mostly restricted to the <a href="/wiki/National_parks_of_Rwanda" title="National parks of Rwanda">three national parks</a>, with <a href="/wiki/Terrace_(agriculture)" class="mw-redirect" title="Terrace (agriculture)">terraced agriculture</a> dominating the rest of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth20063–4_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth20063–4-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Nyungwe_Forest" title="Nyungwe Forest">Nyungwe</a>, the largest remaining tract of forest, contains 200 species of tree as well as <a href="/wiki/Orchid" title="Orchid">orchids</a> and <a href="/wiki/Begonia" title="Begonia">begonias</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKing200711_163-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200711-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> Vegetation in the <a href="/wiki/Volcanoes_National_Park" title="Volcanoes National Park">Volcanoes National Park</a> is mostly <a href="/wiki/Bamboo" title="Bamboo">bamboo</a> and moorland, with small areas of forest.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth20063–4_162-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth20063–4-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> By contrast, Akagera has a <a href="/wiki/Savanna" title="Savanna">savanna</a> ecosystem in which <a href="/wiki/Acacia" title="Acacia">acacia</a> dominates the flora. There are several rare or endangered plant species in Akagera, including <i><a href="/wiki/Markhamia_lutea" title="Markhamia lutea">Markhamia lutea</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Eulophia_guineensis" title="Eulophia guineensis">Eulophia guineensis</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEREMA_(Chapter_5)20093_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEREMA_(Chapter_5)20093-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The greatest diversity of large mammals is found in the three national parks, which are designated conservation areas.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGovernment_of_Rwanda_(II)_166-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGovernment_of_Rwanda_(II)-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Akagera contains typical savanna animals such as giraffes and elephants,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERDB_(III)_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERDB_(III)-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> while Volcanoes is home to an estimated one-third of the worldwide <a href="/wiki/Mountain_gorilla" title="Mountain gorilla">mountain gorilla</a> population.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERDB_(I)2010_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERDB_(I)2010-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> Nyungwe Forest boasts thirteen primate species including <a href="/wiki/Common_chimpanzee" class="mw-redirect" title="Common chimpanzee">common chimpanzees</a> and <a href="/wiki/Black-and-white_colobus" title="Black-and-white colobus">Ruwenzori colobus</a> arboreal monkeys; the Ruwenzori colobus move in groups of up to 400 individuals, the largest troop size of any primate in Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth2006140_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth2006140-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Talk_-_Flickr_-_askmeaks.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Talk_-_Flickr_-_askmeaks.jpg/170px-Talk_-_Flickr_-_askmeaks.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="251" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Talk_-_Flickr_-_askmeaks.jpg/255px-Talk_-_Flickr_-_askmeaks.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Talk_-_Flickr_-_askmeaks.jpg/340px-Talk_-_Flickr_-_askmeaks.jpg 2x" data-file-width="693" data-file-height="1024" /></a><figcaption>Giraffe in Akagera National Park</figcaption></figure>
<p>Rwanda's population of <a href="/wiki/Lion" title="Lion">lions</a> was destroyed in the aftermath of the genocide of 1994, as national parks were turned into camps for displaced people and the remaining animals were poisoned by cattle herders. In June 2015, two South African parks donated seven lions to <a href="/wiki/Akagera_National_Park" title="Akagera National Park">Akagera National Park</a>, reestablishing a lion population in Rwanda.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESmith2015_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith2015-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> The lions were held initially in a fenced-off area of the park, and then collared and released into the wild a month later.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThe_New_Times2015_171-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThe_New_Times2015-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Eighteen endangered black rhinos were brought to Rwanda in 2017 from South Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> The animals adapted very well so in 2019, five more black rhinos were delivered to Akagera National Park from zoos all over Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Similarly, the white rhino population is growing in Rwanda. In 2021, Rwanda received 30 white rhinos from South Africa with the goal of Akagera being a safe breeding ground for the near-threatened species.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are 670 <a href="/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Rwanda" title="List of birds of Rwanda">bird species in Rwanda</a>, with variation between the east and the west.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKing200715_176-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200715-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> Nyungwe Forest, in the west, has 280 recorded species, of which 26 are endemic to the Albertine Rift;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKing200715_176-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200715-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> endemic species include the <a href="/wiki/Rwenzori_turaco" title="Rwenzori turaco">Rwenzori turaco</a> and <a href="/wiki/Handsome_spurfowl" title="Handsome spurfowl">handsome spurfowl</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWCS_177-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWCS-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> Eastern Rwanda, by contrast, features savanna birds such as the <a href="/wiki/Black-headed_gonolek" title="Black-headed gonolek">black-headed gonolek</a> and those associated with swamps and lakes, including <a href="/wiki/Stork" title="Stork">storks</a> and <a href="/wiki/Crane_(bird)" title="Crane (bird)">cranes</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKing200715_176-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200715-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Recent entomological work in the country has revealed a rich diversity of <a href="/wiki/Praying_mantises" class="mw-redirect" title="Praying mantises">praying mantises</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETedrow2015_178-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETedrow2015-178">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> including a new species <i>Dystacta tigrifrutex</i>, dubbed the "bush tiger mantis".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMaynard2014_179-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaynard2014-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rwanda contains three terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Albertine_Rift_montane_forests" title="Albertine Rift montane forests">Albertine Rift montane forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Victoria_Basin_forest-savanna_mosaic" class="mw-redirect" title="Victoria Basin forest-savanna mosaic">Victoria Basin forest-savanna mosaic</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Ruwenzori-Virunga_montane_moorlands" class="mw-redirect" title="Ruwenzori-Virunga montane moorlands">Ruwenzori-Virunga montane moorlands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_180-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-180">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup> The country had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 3.85/10, ranking it 139th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_181-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Rwanda" title="Economy of Rwanda">Economy of Rwanda</a></div>
<p>Rwanda's economy suffered heavily during the 1994 genocide, with widespread loss of life, failure to maintain infrastructure, looting, and neglect of important cash crops. This caused a large drop in GDP and destroyed the country's ability to attract private and external investment.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The economy has since strengthened, with per-capita <a href="/wiki/Nominal_GDP" class="mw-redirect" title="Nominal GDP">nominal GDP</a> estimated at <a href="/wiki/Geary%E2%80%93Khamis_dollar" class="mw-redirect" title="Geary–Khamis dollar">$</a>909.9 in 2022,<sup id="cite_ref-imf_data_182-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-imf_data-182">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> compared with $127 in 1994.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEIMF_(I)_183-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEIMF_(I)-183">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> Major export markets include China, Germany, and the United States.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The economy is managed by the central <a href="/wiki/National_Bank_of_Rwanda" title="National Bank of Rwanda">National Bank of Rwanda</a> and the currency is the <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_franc" title="Rwandan franc">Rwandan franc</a>; in December 2019, the exchange rate was 910 francs to one United States dollar.<sup id="cite_ref-0r_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-0r-184">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda joined the <a href="/wiki/East_African_Community" title="East African Community">East African Community</a> in 2007, and has ratified a plan for <a href="/wiki/Monetary_union" class="mw-redirect" title="Monetary union">monetary union</a> amongst the seven member nations,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAsiimwe2014_185-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAsiimwe2014-185">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> which could eventually lead to a common <a href="/wiki/East_African_shilling#Second_East_African_shilling" title="East African shilling">East African shilling</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELavelle2008_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELavelle2008-186">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rwanda is a country of few natural resources,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2004_144-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2004-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> and the economy is based mostly on <a href="/wiki/Subsistence_agriculture" title="Subsistence agriculture">subsistence agriculture</a> by local farmers using simple tools.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFAO_/_WFP1997_187-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFAO_/_WFP1997-187">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> An estimated 90% of the working population farms, and agriculture constituted an estimated 32.5% of GDP in 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> Farming techniques are basic, with small plots of land and steep slopes.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOur_Africa_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOur_Africa-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> Since the mid-1980s, farm sizes and food production have been decreasing, due in part to the resettlement of displaced people.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWRI2006_189-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWRI2006-189">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2004_144-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2004-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Despite Rwanda's fertile ecosystem, food production often does not keep pace with population growth, and food imports are required.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> However, in recent years with the growth of agriculture, the situation has improved.<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Transporting_bananas.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Transporting_bananas.jpg/220px-Transporting_bananas.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Transporting_bananas.jpg/330px-Transporting_bananas.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Transporting_bananas.jpg/440px-Transporting_bananas.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2560" data-file-height="1920" /></a><figcaption>Rwanda produced 2.6 million tonnes of banana in 2019, its largest cash crop.<sup id="cite_ref-Rwanda_production_in_2019,_by_FAO_191-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rwanda_production_in_2019,_by_FAO-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>Subsistence crops grown in the country include <a href="/wiki/Matoke" title="Matoke">matoke</a> (green bananas), which occupy more than a third of the country's farmland,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOur_Africa_188-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOur_Africa-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Potato" title="Potato">potatoes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bean" title="Bean">beans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sweet_potato" title="Sweet potato">sweet potatoes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">cassava</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wheat" title="Wheat">wheat</a> and <a href="/wiki/Maize" title="Maize">maize</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOur_Africa_188-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOur_Africa-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> Coffee and tea are the major cash crops for export, with the high altitudes, steep slopes and volcanic soils providing favourable conditions.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOur_Africa_188-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOur_Africa-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> Reports have established that more than 400,000 Rwandans make their living from coffee plantation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETumwebaze2016_192-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETumwebaze2016-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> Reliance on agricultural exports makes Rwanda vulnerable to shifts in their prices.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWTO2004_193-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWTO2004-193">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> Animals raised in Rwanda include cows, goats, sheep, pigs, chicken, and rabbits, with geographical variation in the numbers of each.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINAGRI2006_194-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINAGRI2006-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> Production systems are mostly traditional, although there are a few intensive dairy farms around Kigali.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINAGRI2006_194-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINAGRI2006-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> Shortages of land and water, insufficient and poor-quality feed, and regular disease epidemics with insufficient veterinary services are major constraints that restrict output. Fishing takes place on the country's lakes, but stocks are very depleted, and live fish are being imported in an attempt to revive the industry.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENamata2008_195-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENamata2008-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The industrial sector is small, contributing 14.8% of GDP in 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> Products manufactured include cement, agricultural products, small-scale beverages, soap, furniture, shoes, plastic goods, textiles and cigarettes.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda's mining industry is an important contributor, generating US$93&#160;million in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMukaaya2009_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMukaaya2009-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> Minerals mined include <a href="/wiki/Cassiterite" title="Cassiterite">cassiterite</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wolframite" title="Wolframite">wolframite</a>, gold, and <a href="/wiki/Coltan" title="Coltan">coltan</a>, which is used in the manufacture of electronic and communication devices such as mobile phones.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMukaaya2009_196-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMukaaya2009-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDelawala2001_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDelawala2001-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rwanda's <a href="/wiki/Service_sector" class="mw-redirect" title="Service sector">service sector</a> suffered during the <a href="/wiki/Late-2000s_recession" class="mw-redirect" title="Late-2000s recession">late-2000s recession</a> as bank lending, foreign aid projects and investment were reduced.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENantaba2010_198-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENantaba2010-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> The sector rebounded in 2010, becoming the country's largest sector by economic output and contributing 43.6% of the country's GDP.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> Key tertiary contributors include banking and finance, wholesale and retail trade, hotels and restaurants, transport, storage, communication, insurance, real estate, business services and public administration including education and health.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENantaba2010_198-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENantaba2010-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Rwanda" title="Tourism in Rwanda">Tourism</a> is one of the fastest-growing economic resources and became the country's leading foreign exchange earner in 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMukaaya2008_199-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMukaaya2008-199">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> In spite of the genocide's legacy, the country is increasingly perceived internationally as a safe destination.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENielsenSpenceley20106_200-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENielsenSpenceley20106-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> The number of tourist arrivals in 2013 was 864,000 people, up from 504,000 in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(XI)_65-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(XI)-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Revenue from tourism was US$303&#160;million in 2014, up from just US$62&#160;million in 2000.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKT_Press2015_201-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKT_Press2015-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> The largest contributor to this revenue was <a href="/wiki/Mountain_gorilla" title="Mountain gorilla">mountain gorilla</a> tracking, in the Volcanoes National Park;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKT_Press2015_201-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKT_Press2015-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda is one of only three countries in which <a href="/wiki/Mountain_gorilla" title="Mountain gorilla">mountain gorillas</a> can be visited safely; the gorillas attract thousands of visitors per year, who are prepared to pay high prices for permits.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENielsenSpenceley20102_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENielsenSpenceley20102-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> Other attractions include Nyungwe Forest, home to chimpanzees, Ruwenzori colobus and other primates, the resorts of Lake Kivu, and Akagera, a small <a href="/wiki/Savanna_reserve" class="mw-redirect" title="Savanna reserve">savanna reserve</a> in the east of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERDB_(II)_203-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERDB_(II)-203">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media_and_communications">Media and communications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Media and communications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Rwanda" title="Telecommunications in Rwanda">Telecommunications in Rwanda</a></div>
<p>The largest radio and television stations are state-run, and the majority of newspapers are owned by the government.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VII)2015_204-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VII)2015-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> Most Rwandans have access to radio; during the 1994 genocide, the radio station <a href="/wiki/Radio_T%C3%A9l%C3%A9vision_Libre_des_Mille_Collines" title="Radio Télévision Libre des Mille Collines">Radio Télévision Libre des Mille Collines</a> broadcast across the country, and helped to fuel the killings through anti-Tutsi propaganda.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VII)2015_204-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VII)2015-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> As of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the state-run <a href="/wiki/Radio_Rwanda" title="Radio Rwanda">Radio Rwanda</a> is the largest station and the main source of news throughout the country.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VII)2015_204-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VII)2015-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> Television access is limited, with most homes not having their own set.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGasore2014_205-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGasore2014-205">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> The government rolled out <a href="/wiki/Digital_television" title="Digital television">digital television</a> in 2014, and a year later there were seven national stations operating, up from just one in the pre-2014 analogue era.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOpobo2015_206-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOpobo2015-206">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> The press is tightly restricted, and newspapers routinely self-censor to avoid government reprisals.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VII)2015_204-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(VII)2015-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> Nonetheless, publications in Kinyarwanda, English, and French critical of the government are widely available in Kigali. Restrictions were increased in the run-up to the Rwandan presidential election of 2010, with two independent newspapers, <i>Umuseso</i> and <i>Umuvugizi</i>, being suspended for six months by the <a href="/w/index.php?title=High_Media_Council_(Rwanda)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="High Media Council (Rwanda) (page does not exist)">High Media Council</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEReporters_Without_Borders2010_207-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEReporters_Without_Borders2010-207">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The country's oldest telecommunications group, <a href="/wiki/Rwandatel" title="Rwandatel">Rwandatel</a>, went into liquidation in 2011, having been 80% owned by Libyan company <a href="/w/index.php?title=LAP_Green&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="LAP Green (page does not exist)">LAP Green</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMugisha2013_208-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMugisha2013-208">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> The company was acquired in 2013 by <a href="/w/index.php?title=Liquid_Telecom&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Liquid Telecom (page does not exist)">Liquid Telecom</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESouthwood2013_209-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthwood2013-209">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup> a company providing telecommunications and <a href="/wiki/Fibre_optic" class="mw-redirect" title="Fibre optic">fibre optic</a> networks across eastern and southern Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMugwe2013_210-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMugwe2013-210">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> As of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, Liquid Telecom provides <a href="/wiki/Landline" title="Landline">landline</a> service to 30,968 subscribers, with mobile operator <a href="/wiki/MTN_Rwanda" title="MTN Rwanda">MTN Rwanda</a> serving an additional 15,497 fixed line subscribers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERURA20156_211-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERURA20156-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> Landlines are mostly used by government institutions, banks, <a href="/wiki/Non_Government_Organisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Non Government Organisation">NGOs</a> and embassies, with private subscription levels low.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMajyambere2010_212-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMajyambere2010-212">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup> As of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, <a href="/wiki/Mobile_phone" title="Mobile phone">mobile phone</a> penetration in the country is 72.6%,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERURA20155_213-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERURA20155-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> up from 41.6% in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERURA20113_214-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERURA20113-214">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup> MTN Rwanda is the leading provider, with 3,957,986 subscribers, followed by <a href="/wiki/Tigo" class="mw-redirect" title="Tigo">Tigo</a> with 2,887,328, and <a href="/wiki/Bharti_Airtel" title="Bharti Airtel">Bharti Airtel</a> with 1,336,679.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERURA20156_211-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERURA20156-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> Rwandatel has also previously operated a mobile phone network, but the industry regulator revoked its licence in April 2011, following the company's failure to meet agreed investment commitments.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEButera2011_215-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEButera2011-215">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> Internet penetration is low but rising rapidly; in 2015 there were 12.8 internet users per 100 people,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERURA20155_213-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERURA20155-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> up from 2.1 in 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(II)_216-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(II)-216">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, a 2,300-kilometre (1,400&#160;mi) fibre-optic telecommunications network was completed, intended to provide broadband services and facilitate electronic commerce.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEReuters2011_217-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEReuters2011-217">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> This network is connected to <a href="/wiki/SEACOM_(African_cable_system)" title="SEACOM (African cable system)">SEACOM</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Submarine_communications_cable" title="Submarine communications cable">submarine</a> fibre-optic cable connecting communication carriers in southern and eastern Africa. Within Rwanda the cables run along major roads, linking towns around the country.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEReuters2011_217-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEReuters2011-217">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> Mobile provider MTN also runs a <a href="/wiki/Wireless_internet" class="mw-redirect" title="Wireless internet">wireless internet</a> service accessible in most areas of Kigali via pre-paid subscription.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEButera2010_218-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEButera2010-218">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In October 2019, <a href="/wiki/Mara_Corporation" class="mw-redirect" title="Mara Corporation">Mara Corporation</a> launched the first African made smartphone in Rwanda.<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Infrastructure">Infrastructure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Infrastructure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Rwanda" title="Transport in Rwanda">Transport in Rwanda</a>, <a href="/wiki/Energy_in_Rwanda" title="Energy in Rwanda">Energy in Rwanda</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Rwanda" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/63px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/84px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="360" data-file-height="290" /></span></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section needs to be <b>updated</b>. The reason given is: Sanitation access statistics are from 2006.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">March 2023</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table><figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:WaterPumpRwanda.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Photograph depicting one adult and five children filling jerrycans at a rural metal water pump with concrete base, at the bottom of a steep rocky hillside" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/WaterPumpRwanda.jpg/220px-WaterPumpRwanda.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/WaterPumpRwanda.jpg/330px-WaterPumpRwanda.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/WaterPumpRwanda.jpg/440px-WaterPumpRwanda.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="375" /></a><figcaption>Rural water pump</figcaption></figure>
<p>The Rwandan government prioritised funding of water supply development during the 2000s, significantly increasing its share of the national budget.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEIDA2009_220-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEIDA2009-220">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup> This funding, along with donor support, caused a rapid increase in access to safe water; in 2015, 74% of the population had access to safe water,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUmutesi2015_221-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUmutesi2015-221">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> up from about 55% in 2005;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEIDA2009_220-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEIDA2009-220">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup> the government has committed to increasing this to 100% by 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUmutesi2015_221-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUmutesi2015-221">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> The country's water infrastructure consists of urban and rural systems that deliver water to the public, mainly through standpipes in rural areas and private connections in urban areas. In areas not served by these systems, hand pumps and managed springs are used.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINECOFIN200225–26_222-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINECOFIN200225–26-222">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup> Despite rainfall exceeding 750 millimetres (30&#160;in) annually in most of the country,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBerryLewisWilliams1990533_223-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerryLewisWilliams1990533-223">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> little use is made of <a href="/wiki/Rainwater_harvesting" title="Rainwater harvesting">rainwater harvesting</a>, and residents are forced to use water very sparingly, relative to usage in other African countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUmutesi2015_221-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUmutesi2015-221">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> Access to <a href="/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Rwanda" title="Water supply and sanitation in Rwanda">sanitation</a> remains low; the United Nations estimates that in 2006, 34% of urban and 20% of rural dwellers had access to <a href="/wiki/Improved_sanitation" title="Improved sanitation">improved sanitation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(I)20083_224-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(I)20083-224">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> Kigali is one of the cleanest cities in Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> Government policy measures to improve sanitation are limited, focusing only on urban areas.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(I)20083_224-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(I)20083-224">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> The majority of the population, both urban and rural, use public shared <a href="/wiki/Pit_latrine" title="Pit latrine">pit latrines</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(I)20083_224-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(I)20083-224">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rwanda's electricity supply was, until the early 2000s, generated almost entirely from <a href="/wiki/Hydroelectric" class="mw-redirect" title="Hydroelectric">hydroelectric</a> sources; power stations on Lakes <a href="/wiki/Lake_Burera" title="Lake Burera">Burera</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lake_Ruhondo" title="Lake Ruhondo">Ruhondo</a> provided 90% of the country's electricity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20113_226-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20113-226">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup> A combination of below average rainfall and human activity, including the draining of the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Rugezi_wetlands&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Rugezi wetlands (page does not exist)">Rugezi wetlands</a> for cultivation and grazing, caused the two lakes' water levels to fall from 1990 onwards; by 2004 levels were reduced by 50%, leading to a sharp drop in output from the power stations.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20115_227-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20115-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> This, coupled with increased demand as the economy grew, precipitated a shortfall in 2004 and widespread <a href="/wiki/Loadshedding" class="mw-redirect" title="Loadshedding">loadshedding</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20115_227-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20115-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> As an emergency measure, the government installed <a href="/wiki/Diesel_generator" title="Diesel generator">diesel generators</a> north of Kigali; by 2006 these were providing 56% of the country's electricity, but were very costly.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20115_227-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20115-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> The government enacted a number of measures to alleviate this problem, including rehabilitating the Rugezi wetlands, which supply water to Burera and Ruhondo and investing in a scheme to extract methane gas from Lake Kivu, expected in its first phase to increase the country's power generation by 40%.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAfDB2011_228-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAfDB2011-228">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup> Only 18% of the population had access to electricity in 2012, though this had risen from 10.8% in 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(XIII)_229-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(XIII)-229">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> The government's Economic Development and Poverty Reduction Strategy for 2013–18 aims to increase access to electricity to 70% of households by 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBaringanireMalikBanerjee20141_230-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaringanireMalikBanerjee20141-230">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rwanda_electricity_production.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Rwanda_electricity_production.svg/290px-Rwanda_electricity_production.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="247" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Rwanda_electricity_production.svg/435px-Rwanda_electricity_production.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Rwanda_electricity_production.svg/580px-Rwanda_electricity_production.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="639" data-file-height="545" /></a><figcaption>Rwanda electricity production by source</figcaption></figure>
<p>The government has increased investment in the <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Rwanda" title="Transport in Rwanda">transport infrastructure of Rwanda</a> since the 1994 genocide, with aid from the United States, <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, Japan, and others. The transport system consists primarily of the road network, with paved roads between Kigali and most other major cities and towns in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAfDBOECD_Development_Centre2006439_231-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAfDBOECD_Development_Centre2006439-231">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda is linked by road to other countries in the East African Community, namely Uganda, Tanzania, Burundi and <a href="/wiki/Kenya" title="Kenya">Kenya</a>, as well as to the eastern Congolese cities of <a href="/wiki/Goma" title="Goma">Goma</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bukavu" title="Bukavu">Bukavu</a>; the country's most important trade route is the road to the port of <a href="/wiki/Mombasa" title="Mombasa">Mombasa</a> via <a href="/wiki/Kampala" title="Kampala">Kampala</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nairobi" title="Nairobi">Nairobi</a>, which is known as the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Corridor" title="Northern Corridor">Northern Corridor</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETancott2014_232-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETancott2014-232">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup> The principal form of public transport in the country is the <a href="/wiki/Minibus" title="Minibus">minibus</a>, accounting for more than half of all passenger carrying capacity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201334_233-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201334-233">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> Some minibuses, particularly in Kigali,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201367_234-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201367-234">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> operate an unscheduled service, under a <a href="/wiki/Share_taxi" title="Share taxi">shared taxi</a> system,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201332_235-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201332-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> while others run to a schedule, offering express routes between the major cities. There are a smaller number of large buses,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201334_233-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201334-233">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> which operate a scheduled service around the country. The principal private hire vehicle is the <a href="/wiki/Motorcycle_taxi" title="Motorcycle taxi">motorcycle taxi</a>; in 2013 there were 9,609 registered motorcycle taxis in Rwanda, compared with just 579 <a href="/wiki/Taxicab" class="mw-redirect" title="Taxicab">taxicabs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201334_233-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201334-233">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Coach_(vehicle)" class="mw-redirect" title="Coach (vehicle)">Coach</a> services are available to various destinations in neighbouring countries. The country has an <a href="/wiki/Kigali_International_Airport" title="Kigali International Airport">international airport</a> at Kigali that serves several international destinations, the busiest routes being those to <a href="/wiki/JKIA" class="mw-redirect" title="JKIA">Nairobi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Entebbe_International_Airport" title="Entebbe International Airport">Entebbe</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECentre_For_Aviation2014_236-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECentre_For_Aviation2014-236">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup> there is one domestic route, between Kigali and <a href="/wiki/Kamembe_Airport" title="Kamembe Airport">Kamembe Airport</a> near <a href="/wiki/Cyangugu" title="Cyangugu">Cyangugu</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETumwebaze2015_237-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETumwebaze2015-237">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup> In 2017, construction began on the <a href="/wiki/Bugesera_International_Airport" title="Bugesera International Airport">Bugesera International Airport</a>, to the south of Kigali, which will become the country's largest when it opens, complementing the existing Kigali airport.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMININFRA2017_238-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA2017-238">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup> The national carrier is <a href="/wiki/RwandAir" title="RwandAir">RwandAir</a>, and the country is served by seven foreign airlines.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECentre_For_Aviation2014_236-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECentre_For_Aviation2014-236">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup> As of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> the country has no railways, but there is a project underway, in conjunction with Burundi and Tanzania, to extend the Tanzanian <a href="/wiki/Central_Line_(Tanzania)" title="Central Line (Tanzania)">Central Line</a> into Rwanda; the three countries have invited expressions of interest from private firms to form a <a href="/wiki/Public_private_partnership" class="mw-redirect" title="Public private partnership">public private partnership</a> for the scheme.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESenelwa2015_239-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESenelwa2015-239">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup> There is no public water transport between the port cities on Lake Kivu, although a limited private service exists and the government has initiated a programme to develop a full service.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201343_240-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201343-240">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Infrastructure_(Rwanda)" title="Ministry of Infrastructure (Rwanda)">Ministry of Infrastructure</a> is also investigating the feasibility of linking Rwanda to <a href="/wiki/Lake_Victoria" title="Lake Victoria">Lake Victoria</a> via shipping on the <a href="/wiki/Akagera_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Akagera River">Akagera River</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201343_240-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201343-240">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
