<p>As of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the <a href="/wiki/National_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda" title="National Institute of Statistics of Rwanda">National Institute of Statistics of Rwanda</a> estimated Rwanda's population to be 11,262,564,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda2015_242-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda2015-242">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> while the projection for 2022 was 13,246,394.<sup id="cite_ref-RW_243-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RW-243">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup> The 2012 census recorded a population of 10,515,973.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda20143_244-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda20143-244">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup> The population is young: in the 2012 census, 43.3% of the population were aged 15 and under, and 53.4% were between 16 and 64.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda20148_245-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda20148-245">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup> According to the CIA <i><a href="/wiki/World_Factbook" class="mw-redirect" title="World Factbook">World Factbook</a></i>, the annual birth rate is estimated at 40.2 births per 1,000 inhabitants in 2015, and the death rate at 14.9.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The life expectancy is 67.67&#160;years (69.27&#160;years for females and 67.11&#160;years for males), which is the 26th lowest out of 224 countries and territories.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(III)2011_246-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(III)2011-246">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup> The overall sex ratio of the country is 95.9 males per 100 females.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rwandan_children_at_Volcans_National_Park.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Photograph depicting seven rural children, with a straw house and farmland in the background, taken in the Volcanoes National Park in 2005" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Rwandan_children_at_Volcans_National_Park.jpg/220px-Rwandan_children_at_Volcans_National_Park.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Rwandan_children_at_Volcans_National_Park.jpg/330px-Rwandan_children_at_Volcans_National_Park.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Rwandan_children_at_Volcans_National_Park.jpg/440px-Rwandan_children_at_Volcans_National_Park.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1500" data-file-height="1000" /></a><figcaption>Rural children</figcaption></figure>
<p>At 445 inhabitants per square kilometre (1,150/sq&#160;mi),<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda2015_242-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda2015-242">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda's population density is amongst the highest in Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBanda2015_247-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBanda2015-247">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> Historians such as <a href="/wiki/G%C3%A9rard_Prunier" title="Gérard Prunier">Gérard Prunier</a> believe that the 1994 genocide can be partly attributed to the population density.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier19954_52-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier19954-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> The population is predominantly rural, with a few large towns; dwellings are evenly spread throughout the country.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStraus2013215_248-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStraus2013215-248">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup> The only sparsely populated area of the country is the savanna land in the former province of <a href="/wiki/Umutara_Province" title="Umutara Province">Umutara</a> and Akagera National Park in the east.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStreissguth200711_249-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStreissguth200711-249">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup> Kigali is the largest city, with a population of around one million.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKigali_City_250-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKigali_City-250">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup> Its rapidly increasing population challenges its infrastructural development.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPercivalHomer-Dixon1995_251-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPercivalHomer-Dixon1995-251">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEREMA_(Chapter_2)2009_252-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEREMA_(Chapter_2)2009-252">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2012 census, the second largest city is <a href="/wiki/Gisenyi" title="Gisenyi">Gisenyi</a>, which lies adjacent to <a href="/wiki/Lake_Kivu" title="Lake Kivu">Lake Kivu</a> and the Congolese city of <a href="/wiki/Goma" title="Goma">Goma</a>, and has a population of 126,000.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECity_Population2012_253-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECity_Population2012-253">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> Other major towns include <a href="/wiki/Ruhengeri" title="Ruhengeri">Ruhengeri</a>, <a href="/wiki/Butare" title="Butare">Butare</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Muhanga" title="Muhanga">Muhanga</a>, all with populations below 100,000.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECity_Population2012_253-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECity_Population2012-253">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> The urban population rose from 6% of the population in 1990,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPercivalHomer-Dixon1995_251-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPercivalHomer-Dixon1995-251">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup> to 16.6% in 2006;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda201229_254-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda201229-254">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup> by 2011, however, the proportion had dropped slightly, to 14.8%.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda201229_254-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda201229-254">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rwanda has been a unified state since pre-colonial times,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAppiahGates2010450_40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAppiahGates2010450-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> and the population is drawn from just one cultural and linguistic group, the <a href="/wiki/Banyarwanda" title="Banyarwanda">Banyarwanda</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMamdani200252_255-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200252-255">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup> this contrasts with most modern African states, whose borders were drawn by <a href="/wiki/Colonisation_of_Africa" title="Colonisation of Africa">colonial powers</a> and did not correspond to ethnic boundaries or pre-colonial kingdoms.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBoyd19791_256-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoyd19791-256">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup> Within the Banyarwanda people, there are three separate groups, the Hutu, Tutsi and Twa.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier19955_257-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier19955-257">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup> The CIA <i>World Factbook</i> gives estimates that the Hutu made up 84% of the population in 2009, the Tutsi 15% and Twa 1%.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)_69-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The Twa are a pygmy people who descend from Rwanda's earliest inhabitants, but scholars do not agree on the origins of and differences between the Hutu and Tutsi.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMamdani200246–47_258-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200246–47-258">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup> Anthropologist Jean Hiernaux contends that the Tutsi are a separate race, with a tendency towards "long and narrow heads, faces and noses";<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMamdani200247_259-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200247-259">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup> others, such as Villia Jefremovas, believe there is no discernible physical difference and the categories were not historically rigid.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJefremovas1995_260-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJefremovas1995-260">&#91;260&#93;</a></sup> In precolonial Rwanda the Tutsi were the ruling class, from whom the kings and the majority of chiefs were derived, while the Hutu were agriculturalists.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPrunier199511–12_261-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199511–12-261">&#91;261&#93;</a></sup> The current <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Rwanda" class="mw-redirect" title="Government of Rwanda">government</a> discourages the Hutu/Tutsi/Twa distinction, and has removed such classification from identity cards.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeman2010_262-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeman2010-262">&#91;262&#93;</a></sup> The 2002 census was the first since 1933<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKiwuwa201271_263-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKiwuwa201271-263">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup> which did not categorise Rwandan population into the three groups.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAgence_France-Presse2002_264-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgence_France-Presse2002-264">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Rwanda" title="Education in Rwanda">Education in Rwanda</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:OLPC_classroom_teaching.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/OLPC_classroom_teaching.JPG/220px-OLPC_classroom_teaching.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="159" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/OLPC_classroom_teaching.JPG/330px-OLPC_classroom_teaching.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/OLPC_classroom_teaching.JPG/440px-OLPC_classroom_teaching.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4320" data-file-height="3128" /></a><figcaption>Children in a Rwandan primary school, using laptops supplied by the <a href="/wiki/One_Laptop_Per_Child" class="mw-redirect" title="One Laptop Per Child">One Laptop Per Child</a> programme</figcaption></figure>
<p>Prior to 2012, the Rwandan government provided free education in state-run schools for nine years: six years in primary and three years following a common secondary programme.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINEDUC20102_265-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINEDUC20102-265">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup> In 2012, this started to be expanded to 12 years.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliamsAbbottMupenzi2015935_266-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliamsAbbottMupenzi2015935-266">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup> A 2015 study suggests that while enrollment rates in primary schools are "near ubiquity", rates of completion are low and repetition rates high.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliamsAbbottMupenzi2015931_267-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliamsAbbottMupenzi2015931-267">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup> While schooling is fee-free, there is an expectation that parents should contribute to the cost of their children's education by providing them with school supplies, supporting teacher development and making a contribution to school construction. According to the government, these costs should not be a basis for the exclusion of children from education, however.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWilliamsAbbottMupenzi2015935_266-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliamsAbbottMupenzi2015935-266">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup> There are many private schools across the country, some church-run, which follow the same syllabus but charge fees.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200627_268-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200627-268">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup> From 1994 until 2009, secondary education was offered in either French or English; because of the country's increasing ties with the <a href="/wiki/East_African_Community" title="East African Community">East African Community</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth</a>, only the English syllabi are now offered.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMcGreal2009_269-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGreal2009-269">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup> The country has a number of institutions of tertiary education. In 2013, the public <a href="/wiki/University_of_Rwanda" title="University of Rwanda">University of Rwanda</a> (UR) was created out of a merger of the former <a href="/wiki/National_University_of_Rwanda" title="National University of Rwanda">National University of Rwanda</a> and the country's other public higher education institutions.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKoenig2014_270-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKoenig2014-270">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMacGregor2014_271-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMacGregor2014-271">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERutayisire2013_272-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERutayisire2013-272">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, the <a href="/wiki/Gross_enrollment_ratio" class="mw-redirect" title="Gross enrollment ratio">gross enrollment ratio</a> for tertiary education in Rwanda was 7.9%, from 3.6% in 2006.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(III)_273-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(III)-273">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup> The country's <a href="/wiki/Literacy" title="Literacy">literacy</a> rate, defined as those aged 15 or over who can read and write, was 71% in 2009, up from 38% in 1978 and 58% in 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(I)_274-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(I)-274">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Healthcare_in_Rwanda" title="Healthcare in Rwanda">Healthcare in Rwanda</a> and <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Rwanda" title="Health in Rwanda">Health in Rwanda</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Butaro_Hospital.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Photograph depicting a hospital building, with Rwandan flag, viewed from the entrance pathway" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Butaro_Hospital.jpg/220px-Butaro_Hospital.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Butaro_Hospital.jpg/330px-Butaro_Hospital.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Butaro_Hospital.jpg/440px-Butaro_Hospital.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4608" data-file-height="3072" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Butaro_Hospital" title="Butaro Hospital">Butaro Hospital</a> at <a href="/wiki/Burera" class="mw-redirect" title="Burera">Burera</a>, Northern Province</figcaption></figure>
<p>The quality of healthcare in Rwanda has historically been very low, both before and immediately after the 1994 genocide.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDrobacNaughton2014_275-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrobacNaughton2014-275">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup> In 1998, more than one in five children died before their fifth birthday,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(IV)_276-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(IV)-276">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup> often from <a href="/wiki/Malaria" title="Malaria">malaria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBowdler2010_277-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBowdler2010-277">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>President Kagame has made healthcare one of the priorities for the <a href="/wiki/Vision_2020_(Rwanda)" title="Vision 2020 (Rwanda)">Vision 2020</a> development programme,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEvans2014_278-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEvans2014-278">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup> boosting spending on health care to 6.5% of the country's <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">gross domestic product</a> in 2013,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(V)_279-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(V)-279">&#91;279&#93;</a></sup> compared with 1.9% in 1996.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(VI)_280-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(VI)-280">&#91;280&#93;</a></sup> The government has devolved the financing and management of healthcare to local communities, through a system of health insurance providers called <i>mutuelles de santé</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWHO2008_281-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWHO2008-281">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup> The <i>mutuelles</i> were piloted in 1999, and were made available nationwide by the mid-2000s, with the assistance of international development partners.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWHO2008_281-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWHO2008-281">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup> Premiums under the scheme were initially US$2 per annum; since 2011 the rate has varied on a sliding scale, with the poorest paying nothing, and maximum premiums rising to US$8 per adult.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERosenberg2012_282-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERosenberg2012-282">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup> As of 2014<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, more than 90% of the population was covered by the scheme.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(II)2014_283-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(II)2014-283">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup> The government has also set up training institutes including the Kigali Health Institute (KHI), which was established in 1997<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEIMF200034_284-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEIMF200034-284">&#91;284&#93;</a></sup> and is now part of the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Rwanda" title="University of Rwanda">University of Rwanda</a>. In 2005, President Kagame also launched a program known as <i>The Presidents' Malaria Initiative</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-285" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-285">&#91;285&#93;</a></sup> This initiative aimed to help get the most necessary materials for prevention of malaria to the most rural areas of Rwanda, such as mosquito nets and medication.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Life_expectancy_development_in_Rwanda.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Life_expectancy_development_in_Rwanda.svg/220px-Life_expectancy_development_in_Rwanda.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Life_expectancy_development_in_Rwanda.svg/330px-Life_expectancy_development_in_Rwanda.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Life_expectancy_development_in_Rwanda.svg/440px-Life_expectancy_development_in_Rwanda.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>Historical development of life expectancy in Rwanda</figcaption></figure>
<p>In recent years Rwanda has seen improvement on a number of key health indicators. Between 2005 and 2013, life expectancy increased from 55.2 to 64.0,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(VII)_286-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(VII)-286">&#91;286&#93;</a></sup> under-5 mortality decreased from 106.4 to 52.0 per 1,000 live births,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(VIII)_287-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(VIII)-287">&#91;287&#93;</a></sup> and incidence of <a href="/wiki/Tuberculosis" title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a> has dropped from 101 to 69 per 100,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(IX)_288-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(IX)-288">&#91;288&#93;</a></sup> The country's progress in healthcare has been cited by the international media and charities. <i><a href="/wiki/The_Atlantic" title="The Atlantic">The Atlantic</a></i> devoted an article to "Rwanda's Historic Health Recovery".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEmery2013_289-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEmery2013-289">&#91;289&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Partners_In_Health" title="Partners In Health">Partners In Health</a> described the health gains "among the most dramatic the world has seen in the last 50 years".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERosenberg2012_282-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERosenberg2012-282">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite these improvements, however, the country's health profile remains dominated by communicable diseases,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWHO2015_290-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWHO2015-290">&#91;290&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Agency_for_International_Development" title="United States Agency for International Development">United States Agency for International Development</a> has described "significant health challenges",<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(III)2015_291-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(III)2015-291">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup> including the rate of maternal mortality, which it describes as "unacceptably high",<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(III)2015_291-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(III)2015-291">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup> as well as the ongoing <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS" title="HIV/AIDS">HIV/AIDS</a> epidemic.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(III)2015_291-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(III)2015-291">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup> According to the American <a href="/wiki/Centers_for_Disease_Control_and_Prevention" title="Centers for Disease Control and Prevention">Centers for Disease Control and Prevention</a>, travellers to Rwanda are highly recommended to take preventive malaria medication as well as make sure they are up to date with vaccines such as yellow fever.<sup id="cite_ref-292" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-292">&#91;292&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rwanda also has a shortage of medical professionals, with only 0.84 physicians, nurses, and midwives per 1,000 residents.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPartners_In_Health2013_293-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPartners_In_Health2013-293">&#91;293&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme" title="United Nations Development Programme">United Nations Development Programme</a> (UNDP) is monitoring the country's health progress towards <a href="/wiki/Millennium_Development_Goals" title="Millennium Development Goals">Millennium Development Goals</a> 4–6, which relate to healthcare. A mid-2015 UNDP report noted that the country was not on target to meet goal 4 on infant mortality, despite it having "fallen dramatically";<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(II)2015_294-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(II)2015-294">&#91;294&#93;</a></sup> the country is "making good progress" towards goal 5, which is to reduce by three quarters the maternal mortality ratio,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(III)2015_295-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(III)2015-295">&#91;295&#93;</a></sup> while goal 6 is not yet met as <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS_in_Rwanda" title="HIV/AIDS in Rwanda">HIV prevalence</a> has not started falling.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(IV)2015_296-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(IV)2015-296">&#91;296&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Rwanda" title="Religion in Rwanda">Religion in Rwanda</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:RwamaganaChurch.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Photograph depicting the Roman Catholic parish church in Rwamagana, Eastern Province, including the main entrance, façade, the separate bell tower, and dirt forecourt" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/RwamaganaChurch.jpg/220px-RwamaganaChurch.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/RwamaganaChurch.jpg/330px-RwamaganaChurch.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/RwamaganaChurch.jpg/440px-RwamaganaChurch.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2121" data-file-height="1412" /></a><figcaption>Roman Catholic church in <a href="/wiki/Rwamagana" title="Rwamagana">Rwamagana</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The largest faith in Rwanda is <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestantism</a>, but there have been significant changes in the nation's religious demographics since the genocide, with many conversions to <a href="/wiki/Evangelical" class="mw-redirect" title="Evangelical">evangelical</a> Christianity, and, to a lesser degree, <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Rwanda" title="Islam in Rwanda">Islam</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWalkerApril_2004_297-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalkerApril_2004-297">&#91;297&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2012 census, Roman Catholic Christians represented 43.7% of the population, Protestants (excluding <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventists" class="mw-redirect" title="Seventh-day Adventists">Seventh-day Adventists</a>) 37.7%, Seventh-day Adventists 11.8%, and Muslims 2.0%; 0.2% claimed no religious beliefs and 1.3% did not state a religion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda201417_298-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda201417-298">&#91;298&#93;</a></sup> Traditional religion, despite officially being followed by only 0.1% of the population, retains an influence. Many Rwandans view the <a href="/wiki/God_in_Christianity" title="God in Christianity">Christian God</a> as synonymous with the traditional Rwandan God <i><a href="/wiki/Imana" title="Imana">Imana</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWireduAbrahamIreleMenkiti2006236–237_299-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWireduAbrahamIreleMenkiti2006236–237-299">&#91;299&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Rwanda" title="Languages of Rwanda">Languages of Rwanda</a></div>
<p>The country's principal and national language is <a href="/wiki/Kinyarwanda" title="Kinyarwanda">Kinyarwanda</a>, which is virtually spoken by the entire country (98%).<sup id="cite_ref-BC_300-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BC-300">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> The major European languages during the colonial era were <a href="/wiki/German_language" title="German language">German</a>, though it was never taught or widely used, and then <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>, which was introduced by Belgium from 1916 and remained an official and widely spoken language after independence in 1962.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUniversité_Laval2010_301-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUniversité_Laval2010-301">&#91;301&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a> was spoken as well. The return of English-speaking Rwandan refugees in the 1990s<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEUniversité_Laval2010_301-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEUniversité_Laval2010-301">&#91;301&#93;</a></sup> added a new dimension to the country's language policy,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESamuelsonFreedman2010_302-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESamuelsonFreedman2010-302">&#91;302&#93;</a></sup> and the repositioning of Rwanda as a member of the <a href="/wiki/East_African_Community" title="East African Community">East African Community</a> has since increased the importance of English; the educational system was switched from French to English in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-BC_300-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BC-300">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> Kinyarwanda, English, French, and Swahili are all official languages.<sup id="cite_ref-303" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-303">&#91;303&#93;</a></sup> Kinyarwanda is the national language while English is the primary medium of instruction in secondary and tertiary education. <a href="/wiki/Swahili_language" title="Swahili language">Swahili</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Lingua_franca" title="Lingua franca">lingua franca</a> of the <a href="/wiki/East_African_Community" title="East African Community">East African Community</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETabaro2015_304-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETabaro2015-304">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup> is also spoken by some as a second language, particularly returned refugees from Uganda, Kenya, Tanzania, and the Democratic Republic of Congo, and those who live along the border with the DRC.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStanford_University_Swahili_Department_305-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStanford_University_Swahili_Department-305">&#91;305&#93;</a></sup> In 2015, Swahili was introduced as a mandatory subject in secondary schools.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETabaro2015_304-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETabaro2015-304">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup> Inhabitants of Rwanda's <a href="/w/index.php?title=Nkombo_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Nkombo Island (page does not exist)">Nkombo Island</a> speak <a href="/wiki/Shi_language" title="Shi language">Mashi</a>, a language closely related to Kinyarwanda.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENakayima2010_306-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENakayima2010-306">&#91;306&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>French is spoken by slightly under 6% of the population according to the 2012 census and the <i><a href="/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie" title="Organisation internationale de la Francophonie">Organisation internationale de la Francophonie</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-307" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-307">&#91;307&#93;</a></sup>
English was reported to be spoken by 15% of the population in 2009, though the same report found the proportion of French-speakers to be 68%.<sup id="cite_ref-BC_300-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BC-300">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup>
Swahili is spoken by fewer than 1%.<sup id="cite_ref-308" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-308">&#91;308&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Human_rights">Human rights</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Human rights">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Rwanda" title="Human rights in Rwanda">Human rights in Rwanda</a> and <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Rwanda" title="LGBT rights in Rwanda">LGBT rights in Rwanda</a></div>
<p>Homosexuality is generally considered a <a href="/wiki/Taboo" title="Taboo">taboo</a> topic, and there is no significant public discussion of this issue in any region of the country. Some lesbian, gay, bisexual and transgender (LGBT) Rwandans have reported being harassed and blackmailed.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGmünder20071216_309-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGmünder20071216-309">&#91;309&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Spartacus_International_Gay_Guide_2007_310-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Spartacus_International_Gay_Guide_2007-310">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-globalgayz_311-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-globalgayz-311">&#91;311&#93;</a></sup> Same-sex sexual activity is not specifically illegal in Rwanda. Some cabinet-level government officials have expressed support for the rights of LGBT people,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2016_312-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2016-312">&#91;312&#93;</a></sup> however no special legislative protections are afforded to LGBT people,<sup id="cite_ref-Spartacus_International_Gay_Guide_2007_310-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Spartacus_International_Gay_Guide_2007-310">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup> who may be arrested by the police under various laws dealing with public order and morality.<sup id="cite_ref-globalgayz_311-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-globalgayz-311">&#91;311&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Same-sex_marriage" title="Same-sex marriage">Same-sex marriages</a> are not recognized by the state, as the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Rwanda" title="Constitution of Rwanda">constitution</a> provides that "[o]nly civil monogamous marriage between a man and a woman is recognized".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_26_313-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_26-313">&#91;313&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 2006, Human Rights Watch has documented that Rwandan authorities round up and detain <a href="/wiki/Street_children" title="Street children">street children</a>, street vendors, sex workers, homeless people, and beggars, as well as the use of torture in safe houses and other facilities, such as Kami military camp, Kwa Gacinya and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Gikondo_Transit_Center&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Gikondo Transit Center (page does not exist)">Gikondo prison</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-hrw_314-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hrw-314">&#91;314&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Rwanda" title="Culture of Rwanda">Culture of Rwanda</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rwanda_IntoreDancers.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Photograph depicting two male dancers with straw wigs, neck garments, spears and sticks" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Rwanda_IntoreDancers.jpg/220px-Rwanda_IntoreDancers.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="243" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Rwanda_IntoreDancers.jpg/330px-Rwanda_IntoreDancers.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Rwanda_IntoreDancers.jpg/440px-Rwanda_IntoreDancers.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1512" data-file-height="1671" /></a><figcaption>Traditional Rwandan <i><a href="/wiki/Intore" title="Intore">intore</a></i> dancers</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Music_of_Rwanda" title="Music of Rwanda">Music</a> and dance are an integral part of Rwandan ceremonies, festivals, social gatherings and storytelling. The most famous traditional dance is a highly choreographed routine consisting of three components: the <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Umushagiriro&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Umushagiriro (page does not exist)">umushagiriro</a></i>, or cow dance, performed by women;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERwanda_Development_Gateway_315-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERwanda_Development_Gateway-315">&#91;315&#93;</a></sup> the <i><a href="/wiki/Intore" title="Intore">intore</a></i>, or dance of heroes, performed by men;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERwanda_Development_Gateway_315-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERwanda_Development_Gateway-315">&#91;315&#93;</a></sup> and the drumming, also traditionally performed by men, on drums known as <i>ingoma</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERMCA_316-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERMCA-316">&#91;316&#93;</a></sup> The best known dance group is the <a href="/wiki/National_Ballet_of_Rwanda" title="National Ballet of Rwanda">National Ballet</a>. It was established by President Habyarimana in 1974, and performs nationally and internationally.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggs2004_317-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggs2004-317">&#91;317&#93;</a></sup> Traditionally, music is transmitted orally, with styles varying between the social groups. Drums are of great importance; the royal drummers enjoyed high status within the court of the King (<i>Mwami</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle2007135–136_318-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle2007135–136-318">&#91;318&#93;</a></sup> Drummers play together in groups of varying sizes, usually between seven and nine in number.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle2007139_319-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle2007139-319">&#91;319&#93;</a></sup> The country has a growing popular music industry, influenced by African Great Lakes, Congolese, and American music. The most popular genre is <a href="/wiki/Hip_hop" class="mw-redirect" title="Hip hop">hip hop</a>, with a blend of <a href="/wiki/Dancehall" title="Dancehall">dancehall</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rap" class="mw-redirect" title="Rap">rap</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ragga" title="Ragga">ragga</a>, <a href="/wiki/R%26B" class="mw-redirect" title="R&amp;B">R&amp;B</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dance-pop" title="Dance-pop">dance-pop</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMbabazi2008_320-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMbabazi2008-320">&#91;320&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Igiseke.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Photograph depicting a bowl shaped off-white woven basket with tall conical lid and black zigzag pattern" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Igiseke.jpg/170px-Igiseke.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="267" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Igiseke.jpg/255px-Igiseke.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Igiseke.jpg/340px-Igiseke.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1486" data-file-height="2331" /></a><figcaption>Rwandan woven <a href="/wiki/Agaseke" title="Agaseke">agaseke</a> basket</figcaption></figure>
<p>Traditional arts and crafts are produced throughout the country, although most originated as functional items rather than purely for decoration. Woven baskets and bowls are especially common, notably the basket style of the <a href="/wiki/Agaseke" title="Agaseke">agaseke</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200768–70_321-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200768–70-321">&#91;321&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/wiki/Imigongo" title="Imigongo">Imigongo</a></i>, a unique cow dung art, is produced in the southeast of Rwanda, with a history dating back to when the region was part of the independent <a href="/w/index.php?title=Gisaka&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Gisaka (page does not exist)">Gisaka</a> kingdom. The dung is mixed with natural soils of various colours and painted into patterned ridges to form geometric shapes.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth2006243–244_322-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth2006243–244-322">&#91;322&#93;</a></sup> Other crafts include pottery and wood carving.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200631_323-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200631-323">&#91;323&#93;</a></sup> Traditional housing styles make use of locally available materials; circular or rectangular mud homes with grass-thatched roofs (known as <i>nyakatsi</i>) are the most common. The government has initiated a programme to replace these with more modern materials such as corrugated iron.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENtambara2009_324-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENtambara2009-324">&#91;324&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200775_325-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200775-325">&#91;325&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rwanda does not have a long history of written literature, but there is a strong oral tradition ranging from poetry to <a href="/wiki/Folk_stories" class="mw-redirect" title="Folk stories">folk stories</a>. Many of the country's moral values and details of history have been passed down through the generations.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKing2007105_326-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing2007105-326">&#91;326&#93;</a></sup> The most famous Rwandan literary figure was <a href="/wiki/Alexis_Kagame" title="Alexis Kagame">Alexis Kagame</a> (1912–1981), who carried out and published research into oral traditions as well as writing his own poetry.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200629_327-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200629-327">&#91;327&#93;</a></sup> The Rwandan Genocide resulted in the emergence of a literature of witness accounts, essays and fiction by a new generation of writers such as <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Sehene" title="Benjamin Sehene">Benjamin Sehene</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fred_Mfuranzima" title="Fred Mfuranzima">Mfuranzima Fred</a>. A number of films have been produced about the Rwandan Genocide, including the <a href="/wiki/Golden_Globe" class="mw-redirect" title="Golden Globe">Golden Globe</a>-nominated <i><a href="/wiki/Hotel_Rwanda" title="Hotel Rwanda">Hotel Rwanda</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/100_Days_(2001_film)" title="100 Days (2001 film)">100 Days</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Shake_Hands_with_the_Devil_(2007_film)" title="Shake Hands with the Devil (2007 film)">Shake Hands with the Devil</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Sometimes_in_April" title="Sometimes in April">Sometimes in April</a></i>, and <i><a href="/wiki/Shooting_Dogs" title="Shooting Dogs">Shooting Dogs</a></i>, the last four having been filmed in Rwanda and having featured survivors as cast members.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMilmo2006_328-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMilmo2006-328">&#91;328&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFegley201655–56_329-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFegley201655–56-329">&#91;329&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Fourteen regular <a href="/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Rwanda" title="Public holidays in Rwanda">national holidays</a> are observed throughout the year,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGovernment_of_Rwanda_(I)_330-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGovernment_of_Rwanda_(I)-330">&#91;330&#93;</a></sup> with others occasionally inserted by the government. The week following Genocide Memorial Day on 7 April is designated an official week of mourning.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWatsonRenziViggiani201025_331-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatsonRenziViggiani201025-331">&#91;331&#93;</a></sup> The victory for the RPF over the Hutu extremists is celebrated as <a href="/wiki/Liberation_Day" title="Liberation Day">Liberation Day</a> on 4 July. The last Saturday of each month is <i><a href="/wiki/Umuganda" title="Umuganda">umuganda</a></i>, a national morning of mandatory <a href="/wiki/Community_service" title="Community service">community service</a> lasting from 8<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>am to 11<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>am, during which all able bodied people between 18 and 65 are expected to carry out community tasks such as cleaning streets or building homes for vulnerable people.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERwanda_Governance_Board_332-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERwanda_Governance_Board-332">&#91;332&#93;</a></sup> Most normal services close down during <i>umuganda</i>, and public transportation is limited.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERwanda_Governance_Board_332-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERwanda_Governance_Board-332">&#91;332&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cuisine_of_Rwanda" class="mw-redirect" title="Cuisine of Rwanda">Cuisine of Rwanda</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ugali_and_cabbage.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Ugali_and_cabbage.jpg/220px-Ugali_and_cabbage.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="193" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Ugali_and_cabbage.jpg/330px-Ugali_and_cabbage.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Ugali_and_cabbage.jpg/440px-Ugali_and_cabbage.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2183" data-file-height="1912" /></a><figcaption>A plate of <i><a href="/wiki/Ugali" title="Ugali">ugali</a></i> and <a href="/wiki/Cabbage" title="Cabbage">cabbage</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The cuisine of Rwanda is based on local <a href="/wiki/Staple_food" title="Staple food">staple foods</a> produced by subsistence agriculture such as bananas, plantains (known as <i><a href="/wiki/Ibitoke" class="mw-redirect" title="Ibitoke">ibitoke</a></i>), <a href="/wiki/Pulse_(legume)" class="mw-redirect" title="Pulse (legume)">pulses</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sweet_potato" title="Sweet potato">sweet potatoes</a>, beans, and <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">cassava</a> (manioc).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200781_333-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200781-333">&#91;333&#93;</a></sup> Many Rwandans do not eat meat more than a few times a month.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200781_333-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200781-333">&#91;333&#93;</a></sup> For those who live near lakes and have access to fish, <a href="/wiki/Tilapia" title="Tilapia">tilapia</a> is popular.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200781_333-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200781-333">&#91;333&#93;</a></sup> The potato, thought to have been introduced to Rwanda by German and Belgian <a href="/wiki/Second_European_colonization_wave_(19th_century%E2%80%9320th_century)" class="mw-redirect" title="Second European colonization wave (19th century–20th century)">colonialists</a>, is very popular.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200713_334-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200713-334">&#91;334&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Ugali" title="Ugali">Ugali</a>, locally known as <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ubugari&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ubugari (page does not exist)">Ubugari</a></i> (or <i>umutsima</i>) is common, a paste made from cassava or maize and water to form a <a href="/wiki/Porridge" title="Porridge">porridge</a>-like consistency that is eaten throughout the African Great Lakes.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAuzias200774_335-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAuzias200774-335">&#91;335&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Isombe&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Isombe (page does not exist)">Isombe</a></i> is made from mashed cassava leaves and can be served with dried fish, rice, ugali, potatoes etc.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200713_334-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200713-334">&#91;334&#93;</a></sup> Lunch is usually a buffet known as <i>mélange</i>, consisting of the above staples and sometimes meat.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200654–55_336-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200654–55-336">&#91;336&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Brochette" class="mw-redirect" title="Brochette">Brochettes</a> are the most popular food when eating out in the evening, usually made from goat but sometimes <a href="/wiki/Tripe" title="Tripe">tripe</a>, beef, or fish.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200654–55_336-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200654–55-336">&#91;336&#93;</a></sup> In rural areas, many bars have a brochette seller responsible for tending and slaughtering the goats, skewering and <a href="/wiki/Barbecue" title="Barbecue">barbecuing</a> the meat, and serving it with grilled bananas.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnyango2010_337-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnyango2010-337">&#91;337&#93;</a></sup> Milk, particularly in a fermented yoghurt form called <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ikivuguto&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ikivuguto (page does not exist)">ikivuguto</a></i>, is a common drink throughout the country.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENzabuheraheza2005_338-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENzabuheraheza2005-338">&#91;338&#93;</a></sup> Other drinks include a traditional beer called Ikigage made from sorghum and <i><a href="/wiki/Urwagwa" class="mw-redirect" title="Urwagwa">urwagwa</a></i>, made from bananas, and a soft drink called Umutobe which is banana juice; these popular drinks feature in traditional rituals and ceremonies.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200713_334-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200713-334">&#91;334&#93;</a></sup> The major drinks manufacturer in Rwanda is <a href="/wiki/Bralirwa" class="mw-redirect" title="Bralirwa">Bralirwa</a>, which was established in the 1950s, a Heineken partner, and is now listed on the <a href="/wiki/Rwandan_Stock_Exchange" class="mw-redirect" title="Rwandan Stock Exchange">Rwandan Stock Exchange</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBralirwa_(I)_339-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBralirwa_(I)-339">&#91;339&#93;</a></sup> Bralirwa manufactures <a href="/wiki/Soft_drink" title="Soft drink">soft drink</a> products from <a href="/wiki/The_Coca-Cola_Company" title="The Coca-Cola Company">The Coca-Cola Company</a>, under licence, including <a href="/wiki/Coca-Cola" title="Coca-Cola">Coca-Cola</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fanta" title="Fanta">Fanta</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sprite_(soft_drink)" class="mw-redirect" title="Sprite (soft drink)">Sprite</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBralirwa_(II)_340-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBralirwa_(II)-340">&#91;340&#93;</a></sup> and a range of beers including <a href="/wiki/Primus_beer" class="mw-redirect" title="Primus beer">Primus</a>, <a href="/wiki/M%C3%BCtzig_beer" class="mw-redirect" title="Mützig beer">Mützig</a>, <a href="/wiki/Amstel_Brewery" title="Amstel Brewery">Amstel</a>, and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Turbo_King&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Turbo King (page does not exist)">Turbo King</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBralirwa_(III)_341-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBralirwa_(III)-341">&#91;341&#93;</a></sup> In 2009 a new brewery, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Brasseries_des_Mille_Collines&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Brasseries des Mille Collines (page does not exist)">Brasseries des Mille Collines</a> (BMC) opened, manufacturing <a href="/wiki/Skol" title="Skol">Skol</a> beer and a local version known as <a href="/w/index.php?title=Skol_Gatanu&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Skol Gatanu (page does not exist)">Skol Gatanu</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENgarambe2012_342-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENgarambe2012-342">&#91;342&#93;</a></sup> BMC is now owned by Belgian company <a href="/wiki/Unibra" title="Unibra">Unibra</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECraig2012_343-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECraig2012-343">&#91;343&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/East_African_Breweries" title="East African Breweries">East African Breweries</a> also operate in the country, importing <a href="/wiki/Guinness" title="Guinness">Guinness</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tusker_(beer)" title="Tusker (beer)">Tusker</a>, and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Bell_(beer)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Bell (beer) (page does not exist)">Bell</a>, as well as <a href="/wiki/Whisky" title="Whisky">whisky</a> and <a href="/wiki/Distilled_beverage" class="mw-redirect" title="Distilled beverage">spirits</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKezio-Musoke2014_344-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKezio-Musoke2014-344">&#91;344&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Rwanda" title="Sport in Rwanda">Sport in Rwanda</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:MTB_cycling_2012_Olympics_M_cross-country_RWA_Adrien_Niyonshuti.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/MTB_cycling_2012_Olympics_M_cross-country_RWA_Adrien_Niyonshuti.jpg/220px-MTB_cycling_2012_Olympics_M_cross-country_RWA_Adrien_Niyonshuti.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/MTB_cycling_2012_Olympics_M_cross-country_RWA_Adrien_Niyonshuti.jpg/330px-MTB_cycling_2012_Olympics_M_cross-country_RWA_Adrien_Niyonshuti.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/MTB_cycling_2012_Olympics_M_cross-country_RWA_Adrien_Niyonshuti.jpg/440px-MTB_cycling_2012_Olympics_M_cross-country_RWA_Adrien_Niyonshuti.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4272" data-file-height="2848" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Adrien_Niyonshuti" title="Adrien Niyonshuti">Adrien Niyonshuti</a>, "one of the most famous people in Rwanda",<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECyclingNews.com2012_345-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECyclingNews.com2012-345">&#91;345&#93;</a></sup> competing in the <a href="/wiki/Cross-country_mountain_biking" class="mw-redirect" title="Cross-country mountain biking">cross-country mountain biking</a> event at the 2012 Summer Olympics</figcaption></figure>
<p>The Rwandan government, through its Sports Development Policy, promotes sport as a strong avenue for "development and peace building",<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMINISPOC201218_346-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINISPOC201218-346">&#91;346&#93;</a></sup> and the government has made commitments to advancing the use of sport for a variety of development objectives, including education.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMcCrackenColucci201486–90_347-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCrackenColucci201486–90-347">&#91;347&#93;</a></sup> The most popular sports in Rwanda are <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">association football</a>, <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">basketball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Athletics_(sport)" class="mw-redirect" title="Athletics (sport)">athletics</a> and <a href="/wiki/Paralympic_sports" title="Paralympic sports">Paralympic sports</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENdengeye2014125–128_348-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENdengeye2014125–128-348">&#91;348&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cricket" title="Cricket">Cricket</a> has been growing in popularity,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAglietti2014_349-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAglietti2014-349">&#91;349&#93;</a></sup> as a result of refugees returned from Kenya, where they had learned to play the game.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(XI)2014_350-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_News_(XI)2014-350">&#91;350&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cycling" title="Cycling">Cycling</a>, traditionally seen largely as a mode of transport in Rwanda, is also growing in popularity <a href="/wiki/Cycle_sport" title="Cycle sport">as a sport</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHoyeSmithNicholsonStewart2015206_351-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoyeSmithNicholsonStewart2015206-351">&#91;351&#93;</a></sup> and Team Rwanda have been the subject of a book, <i>Land of Second Chances: The Impossible Rise of Rwanda's Cycling Team</i> and a film, <i><a href="/wiki/Rising_from_Ashes_(film)" title="Rising from Ashes (film)">Rising from Ashes</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERobbins2013_352-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERobbins2013-352">&#91;352&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWillgoss2014_353-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWillgoss2014-353">&#91;353&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:GCS_Drone_Shot-YT.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/GCS_Drone_Shot-YT.jpg/220px-GCS_Drone_Shot-YT.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/GCS_Drone_Shot-YT.jpg/330px-GCS_Drone_Shot-YT.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/GCS_Drone_Shot-YT.jpg/440px-GCS_Drone_Shot-YT.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2989" data-file-height="2242" /></a><figcaption>The Gahanga Cricket Stadium</figcaption></figure>
<p>Rwandans have been competing at the <a href="/wiki/Olympic_Games" title="Olympic Games">Olympic Games</a> since 1984,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_Sport_(I)2012_354-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_Sport_(I)2012-354">&#91;354&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/Paralympic_Games" title="Paralympic Games">Paralympic Games</a> since 2004.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEInternational_Paralympic_Committee2015_355-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternational_Paralympic_Committee2015-355">&#91;355&#93;</a></sup> The country sent seven competitors to the <a href="/wiki/2012_Summer_Olympics" title="2012 Summer Olympics">2012 Summer Olympics</a> in London, representing it in athletics, <a href="/wiki/Swimming_(sport)" title="Swimming (sport)">swimming</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mountain_biking" title="Mountain biking">mountain biking</a> and <a href="/wiki/Judo" title="Judo">judo</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_Sport_(I)2012_354-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_Sport_(I)2012-354">&#91;354&#93;</a></sup> and 15 competitors to the <a href="/wiki/2012_Summer_Paralympics" title="2012 Summer Paralympics">London Summer Paralympics</a> to compete in athletics, <a href="/wiki/Paralympic_powerlifting" title="Paralympic powerlifting">powerlifting</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sitting_volleyball" title="Sitting volleyball">sitting volleyball</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEInternational_Paralympic_Committee2015_355-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternational_Paralympic_Committee2015-355">&#91;355&#93;</a></sup> The country has also participated in the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_Games" title="Commonwealth Games">Commonwealth Games</a> since joining the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth</a> in 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBBC_Sport_(II)2010_356-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBBC_Sport_(II)2010-356">&#91;356&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEOffice_of_the_Prime_Minister2014_357-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOffice_of_the_Prime_Minister2014-357">&#91;357&#93;</a></sup> The country's <a href="/wiki/Rwanda_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Rwanda national basketball team">national basketball team</a> has been growing in prominence since the mid-2000s, with the men's team qualifying for the final stages of the <a href="/wiki/African_Basketball_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="African Basketball Championship">African Basketball Championship</a> four times in a row since 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBishumba2015_358-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBishumba2015-358">&#91;358&#93;</a></sup> The country bid unsuccessfully to host the <a href="/wiki/2013_African_Basketball_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="2013 African Basketball Championship">2013 tournament</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMackay2009_359-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMackay2009-359">&#91;359&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEInternational_Basketball_Federation2011_360-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternational_Basketball_Federation2011-360">&#91;360&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda's <a href="/wiki/Rwanda_national_football_team" title="Rwanda national football team">national football team</a> has appeared in the <a href="/wiki/African_Cup_of_Nations" class="mw-redirect" title="African Cup of Nations">African Cup of Nations</a> once, in the <a href="/wiki/2004_African_Cup_of_Nations" title="2004 African Cup of Nations">2004 edition</a> of the tournament,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECarlin2003_361-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECarlin2003-361">&#91;361&#93;</a></sup> but narrowly failed to advance beyond the group stages.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECopnall2004_362-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECopnall2004-362">&#91;362&#93;</a></sup> The team have failed to qualify for the competition since, and have never qualified for the <a href="/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup" title="FIFA World Cup">World Cup</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMontague201467_363-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontague201467-363">&#91;363&#93;</a></sup> Rwanda's highest domestic football competition is the <a href="/wiki/Rwanda_National_Football_League" class="mw-redirect" title="Rwanda National Football League">Rwanda National Football League</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMugabeKamasa2014_364-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMugabeKamasa2014-364">&#91;364&#93;</a></sup> as of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the dominant team is <a href="/wiki/APR_FC" class="mw-redirect" title="APR FC">APR FC</a> of Kigali, having won 13 of the last 17 championships.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchöggl2015_365-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchöggl2015-365">&#91;365&#93;</a></sup> Rwandan clubs participate in the <a href="/wiki/Kagame_Interclub_Cup" title="Kagame Interclub Cup">Kagame Interclub Cup</a> for Central and East African teams, sponsored since 2002 by President Kagame.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECECAFA_366-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECECAFA-366">&#91;366&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1132942124">.mw-parser-output .portalbox{padding:0;margin:0.5em 0;display:table;box-sizing:border-box;max-width:175px;list-style:none}.mw-parser-output .portalborder{border:solid #aaa 1px;padding:0.1em;background:#f9f9f9}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-entry{display:table-row;font-size:85%;line-height:110%;height:1.9em;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-image{display:table-cell;padding:0.2em;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-link{display:table-cell;padding:0.2em 0.2em 0.2em 0.3em;vertical-align:middle}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .portalleft{clear:left;float:left;margin:0.5em 1em 0.5em 0}.mw-parser-output .portalright{clear:right;float:right;margin:0.5em 0 0.5em 1em}}</style><ul role="navigation" aria-label="Portals" class="noprint portalbox portalborder portalright">

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Citations">Citations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Rwanda&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Citations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-columns references-column-width" style="column-width: 20em;">
